# Appendix D — Spec, decision and ticket ledger

One section per document group, as inventoried.

## context-glossary

**Destination:** "This project is a faithful re-implementation of Chris Nesbitt-Smith's (CNS) Policy as [Versioned] Code thesis onto Flux CD. Source material: the talk, the original Medium post, the later 'mea culpa' blog post, and two reference GitHub orgs (example-policy-org, policy-as-versioned-code)." Also: "Fidelity = 'faithful to intent.' Reproduce the thesis and its ethos 1:1, but let Flux do natively what the 2022 implementation had to hack ... The PRD targets this faithful-to-intent floor; a separate modern-reference report documents the fuller 'north star' design." (CONTEXT.md)

### Decisions

| Id | Title | Status | Gist | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-0001 | Transport: signed git tags, keyless (gitsign) | referenced only (not read from ADR file; described in CONTEX | Policy distributed as semver git tags, signed keyless with sigstore/gitsign, consumed via a Flux GitRepository pinned on spec.ref.tag AND spec.ref.commit. Known limitation accepted: no Flux-native verified-source admission gate on the floor (Flux verify is PGP | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0002 | Adoption cadence: pinned everywhere + Renovate PR | referenced only | Consumers and clusters pin exact tags; new versions land only via a reviewed Renovate PR (automerge:false). Live semver ranges rejected. Adoption cadence and enforcement action are independent axes. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0003 | Engine: Kyverno; policies authored as CEL ValidatingPolicy | referenced only | Kyverno ValidatingPolicy with validationActions: Audit = lane-keeping; Deny = gate. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0004 | Cloud plane: fork/harvest collie | referenced only | Cloud plane built by harvesting ControlPlane's collie (OSCAL catalogue + policy intent as data); its generator/Lula toolchain and bootstrap dropped. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0005 | Install/fleet layer: ControlPlane Flux Operator | referenced only | FluxInstance + ResourceSet matrix used as install + fleet layer; thesis stays vanilla-Flux-expressible. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0006 | Deterministic policy — no time conditions | referenced only | Policy bodies contain no time-conditional logic; same manifest + same policy version always evaluates the same. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0007 | Agent-assisted editorial governance | referenced only | Human-governance layer realised as editorial review (reviewed PR), supported by the agent governance layer. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0009 | OSCAL attestation via C2P | referenced only | C2P's result2oscal consumes Kyverno PolicyReports to produce OSCAL assessment-results — carrier of the 'measurable' pillar's control attestation. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0010 | Sunset: scheduled proposals, not scheduled application | referenced only | A fleet's array entry may carry a sunset date; on that date a machine opens a retirement PR a human must merge — nothing timed ever changes an admission verdict on its own. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0012 | Composed artefact: self-signed, pinned SHA | referenced only | Adopter signs its composed artefact as any publisher signs an artefact, carrying each parent's resolved commit SHA; a verifier re-renders and checks byte-for-byte. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0013 | Regulator publishes baselines, adopter selects | referenced only | Regulator publishes named, signed OSCAL baselines; adopter selects one and may add controls but never remove one. Control id identity is the bare catalogue id, exact-string resolution. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0014 | Unclaimed is caged; governed namespace requires claim | referenced only | Orphan guard denies admission of a workload claiming a retired version; a pod with no claim is out of scope unless in a governed namespace, where unclaimed is denied on CREATE only (UPDATE excluded for de-posturing). | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0015 | Adopter runs the proposer and it opens the PR | referenced only | Proposer runs in the adopter's repo against its own composed artefact; bounded by confidence floor, rate limit, rejection ledger; no merge()/approve(); starts on merged version-pin bump or human dispatch, never a clock. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0016 | A subclass never restates a Mutate | referenced only | Restatement applies only to ValidatingPolicy on the Audit<Deny ladder; a composition restating a MutatingPolicy/GeneratingPolicy is refused. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0017 | A control claim belongs to whoever ships the implementation | referenced only | A control claim is a signed statement in a party's OSCAL component-definition that a policy it ships evidences a control id; a party may never claim against another party's policy. | CONTEXT.md |
| ADR-0018 | The namespace manifest is the governed declaration | referenced only | The governed label on the adopter's Namespace manifest is the declaration, signed under the same tag as the composed artefact; only the adopter adds a governed namespace, by hand — the proposer never proposes one. | CONTEXT.md |

### Rejections and supersessions

- "Exemption — A banned concept. There are none, ever. ... The estate shipped an exemptions ledger that contradicted this; it is removed — see `.scratch/govern-what-you-dont-control/issues/05-remove-the-exemption-ledger.md`." (CONTEXT.md)
- "Reproduce the thesis and its ethos 1:1, but let Flux do natively what the 2022 implementation had to hack (the scaffolding that only existed because GitOps tooling couldn't yet express 'versioned policy as a live dependency' is dropped, not preserved)." (CONTEXT.md)
- "No bespoke tooling. ... no wrapper, no re-implemented `policy-checker`. The 2022 bash/Docker checker is deleted, not ported." (CONTEXT.md)
- "We harvest its reusable IP ... and rebuild the cloud plane natively; its generator/Lula/bootstrap are dropped (ADR-0004)." (CONTEXT.md, re: collie)
- "a weakening is never an override and never an exemption, it is a declared inability that is caged and priced" (CONTEXT.md, on Restatement)
- "a removal is an exemption by another name, and a control the adopter cannot meet is caged and priced, not dropped" (CONTEXT.md, on Baseline)

### Open items

- "The last-mile problem ... An explicitly acknowledged open problem, not something the system claims to solve." (CONTEXT.md)
- "Known limitation (accepted): Flux GitRepository.spec.verify is PGP-only and cannot verify gitsign signatures today, so there is no Flux-native verified-source admission gate on the floor. ... pending upstream fluxcd/source-controller#1068 (a tracked project action — see docs/upstream/)." (CONTEXT.md)
- "Deferred to north-star (need OCI): signed attestations carrying the 'why', and SBOM." (CONTEXT.md)
- "Compliance / measurable ... In the original this was a proxy ('a GitHub PR search away'). See open question on proxy-vs-ground-truth." (CONTEXT.md) — referenced but not resolved in this file
- "A real-cloud e2e (live RDS/S3, optional LocalStack provisioning, C2P over realized state) is optional and documented." (CONTEXT.md)

**Reviewer notes:** CONTEXT.md self-describes as "a glossary, not a spec — no implementation details," so it has no build tickets and no direct implementation claims; all ADR content here is CONTEXT.md's own paraphrase of decisions, since the ADR files themselves were outside the paths given for this group. docs/agents/domain.md and docs/agents/issue-tracker.md are process/meta docs (how to consume domain docs, where issues live) with no decisions or claims of their own. CLAUDE.md is a two-line pointer to those same docs/agents/*.md files. No status lines or dates exist anywhere in these four files (prose-glossary format, not ticket format) — the "status" field on each decision is filled with "referenced only" rather than a verbatim status line, since none exists. The exemption-ledger removal is the only concrete cross-reference to another effort's artifact (.scratch/govern-what-you-dont-control/issues/05-remove-the-exemption-ledger.md) — worth checking against that group's own inventory.

## docs-history-showtell

**Destination:** "policy — the rules that mitigate risk — should be treated like any other software dependency: versioned, distributed, tested, updated by reviewed pull request" ... "most of an enterprise's real policy surface is *lane-keeping*, continuous corrective guidance, and a system that only knows how to say \"no\" repeats the original mistake. This repo re-implements that corrected thesis faithfully, on Flux CD, as a real, runnable, live-verified system — not a slide deck." (docs/HISTORY.md). SHOW-AND-TELL.md restates it as: "treat a body of governance policy like a software dependency — semantically versioned, signed, pinned, distributed, unit-tested, and updated by reviewed PR — *not* primarily a deploy-time gate," scored against seven "-ables": visible, communicable, consumable, testable, usable, updatable, measurable.

### Decisions

| Id | Title | Status | Gist | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|

Splitting into a components org vs a model-consuming org is called out as a real, separate d | docs/HISTORY.md |
| deferred-fresh-org-redeploy | Fresh-org redeploy ("clean history") | Deferred — named as the project's eventual sequel | A viewer asked for clean history during the demo; the answer is not rewriting this org's history but a future from-scratch redeploy into a fresh org, proving reproducibility, while this org stays as the record of how it was actually built, thrashing included.  | docs/HISTORY.md |

If wanted  | .scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md |
| conclusions-item6-extract-gate | Extract pr-gate-check.sh into a separate reusable Action/repo | DEFER (YAGNI today) | Exactly one real consumer (fleet) exists today; extracting now creates a governed dependency with no second consumer to justify it. Revisit when a second config repo appears. | .scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md |
| conclusions-item12-sunset-times | Policy versions should have a sunset time | RECONCILABLE with ADR-0006 via reframe — needs a new ADR (la | ADR-0006 rejects timed enforcement changes; fix is a sunset field on the fleet array entry (adoption-scoped, not part of the immutable policy release), dashboard time-to-sunset, governance-agent escalation, and at the date a machine opens a retirement PR a hum | .scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md; docs/HISTORY.md |
| adr-0010-sunset-cited | ADR-0010: sunset scheduled proposals, not application | Adopted (cited, not reproduced in this group) | Sunset is framed as a scheduled proposal (a machine-opened PR a human must merge) never a scheduled application — extends ADR-0006's determinism boundary deliberately, leaning on ADR-0002's 'machine-opened, human-merged' precedent. | docs/HISTORY.md |

### Rejections and supersessions

- "the estate around the mechanism was a cardboard cutout. Three consumer apps were identical nginx pods in one monorepo — no real dependency trees, no real teams, no real cadences." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- "The old apps monorepo is archived, not deleted — its README points forward to the five repos that replaced it." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- "RECOMMEND AGAINST. Offer alternative." on history rewrite / repo recreation, with "My recommendation remains no." (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md, item 4)
- "DEFER (YAGNI today)." on extracting the PR gate into a reusable Action — "Revisit when a second config repo appears." (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md, item 6)
- "No history rewrite happens *in place*, ever." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- Ticket 12's headline claim that ledger would be "worst-in-class on both staleness axes" turned out true on the policy-version axis and false on the vulnerability axis — ledger has fewer live CVEs (22) than reports (188) or storefront (146); "Left as the genuinely interesting, slightly inconvenient finding it is, not reshaped to fit the original thesis." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- A prior 'correction' claiming two governance-issue checkbox templates use identical wording was itself wrong — "only one of the three lines actually matches, and the other two don't just differ in wording, they swap which answer means what." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- "A second adversarial pass caught an earlier draft of this very paragraph repeating the wrong, broader claim — 'never actually merged' — even after ticket 09's own file had already corrected it; fixed here to match." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- "this document briefly asserted the entire project's history spans 2026-07-14 to 2026-07-18, when only the real-estate epic does; the hub repo's actual first commit is 2026-06-06, five weeks earlier." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- "Ticket 16's ADR-0005 citation for flux-operator's chart version pin was wrong — the pin lives in fleet's own up.sh, not the ADR, which never mentions a version number." (docs/HISTORY.md)
- ADR-0010's "on the date itself, a machine opens a retirement PR" was found to be "aspirational, not automated" before fleet#58 fixed it. (docs/HISTORY.md)

### Open items

- Two-org split (components org vs consuming model org) — deferred, not acted on this epic (docs/HISTORY.md)
- Fresh-org redeploy / reproducibility sequel — deferred to a future project phase (docs/HISTORY.md)
- Sunset retirement-PR path blocked on one org setting: 'Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests' OFF org-wide, needs an admin toggle (docs/SHOW-AND-TELL.md)
- Known upstream issue: flux-operator's ResourceSet controller occasionally flaps policy-1.0.0's derived objects (self-heals, never observed to admit a non-compliant workload) — documented, not fixed (docs/SHOW-AND-TELL.md)
- Extract pr-gate-check.sh into a reusable Action — deferred until a second consumer repo exists (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md, item 6)
- Release artifacts (rendered bundle + SHA256SUMS) — endorsed-small but scoped down, not confirmed built in this group's sources (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md, item 3)
- Renovate onboarding PRs expected on repos without local renovate.json (policy, apps, governance-agent, hub, cloud); per-repo vs org-level config decision not resolved in this group's sources (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md, item 13b)
- Fact-check pending at time of CONCLUSIONS.md: kyverno CLI's support for ValidatingPolicy in apply against dumped cluster resources, needed for the readiness-collector design; not explicitly shown closed in this group's sources (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md, item 10)
- Sunset-ADR needed before the sunset-time feature could proceed; HISTORY.md shows ADR-0010 exists but CONCLUSIONS.md itself never records that closure (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md)
- History rewrite (item 4) explicitly left open pending the user's own decision after grilling, gated on its own dedicated confirmation (.scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md)

**Reviewer notes:** docs/HISTORY.md is unusually self-referential: it documents at least four in-place self-corrections to its own earlier drafts (the 'day-old not year-old' claim, the over-corrected 'entire project spans 2026-07-14 to 2026-07-18' claim, ticket 09's 'never actually merged' broad claim, ticket 16's wrong ADR-0005 citation). docs/SHOW-AND-TELL.md's footer states audits ran '5 waves this session,' while docs/HISTORY.md's own narrative names only two explicit passes ('a second wave' and 'a later pass') plus the original build — the exact count of 5 vs the passes actually narrated in HISTORY.md is not reconciled in this group's sources. .scratch/demo-feedback/CONCLUSIONS.md is explicitly pre-grilling and provisional; several of its research-pending items (readiness-collector's kyverno CLI support, item 13's onboarding-PR questions) are not explicitly closed out anywhere in this same group's sources, even though docs/HISTORY.md describes the readiness-collector and Renovate as built/live — treat CONCLUSIONS.md as superseded-in-substance but not formally closed within these four files. NOTES.md item 12 itself flags an internal tension with ADR-0006 that CONCLUSIONS.md resolves via a reframe and HISTORY.md later cites as resolved by ADR-0010 — the resolution chain runs across all three files. The ledger CVE-count reversal directly contradicts ticket 12's original headline claim from the real-estate epic (not in this file set, only referenced) — kept deliberately as an honest, inconvenient finding. docs/SHOW-AND-TELL.md is written as a live runbook presupposing the estate is already fully built, describing capability as fact in present tense throughout, whereas docs/HISTORY.md flags which of those capabilities were fixed only after adversarial audits found them missing or broken — a reviewer relying only on SHOW-AND-TELL.md would miss the honesty caveats HISTORY.md records for the same features (cluster-state wiring, OSCAL mis-attribution, orphan guard cloud coverage).

## faithful-floor

**Destination:** No standalone thesis/mission statement file exists in this issues directory; the destination is expressed implicitly across all 26 tickets as building out the 'faithful-floor' epic: a versioned-policy-as-code control plane where policy is released as signed semver tags (ADR-0001), consumed via Flux GitOps ResourceSets across coexisting versions, enforced by Kyverno CEL ValidatingPolicies in Audit (lane-keeping) and Deny (gate) tiers, extended to a Crossplane cloud plane, made measurable via OSCAL/C2P and a CIO dashboard, updatable via a single Renovate surface, usable via native-CLI local reproduction, communicable via release notes/alerts, and governed by an advisory-metadata + editorial-review process with a bounded agent-governance layer that only ever proposes review, never edits enforcement. Ticket 10 states the throughline explicitly: 'the estate's gate strength equals its weakest installed version, so retiring is a security action' and that this closes 'the silent-ungovernance gap of the 2022 implementation.' Ticket 13 states the 2022 baseline being retired: 'The 2022 bash/Docker checker stays deleted.'

### Rejections and supersessions

- Ticket 08, 2026-07-18 correction: 'the paragraph below was an earlier, stale snapshot ... It also described app2/app3 (in the old apps monorepo) as "already working" in the present tense; that stopped being true 2026-07-16, when real-estate ticket 08 deleted app1/app2/app3 from the live cluster and archived the apps repo entirely ... superseding them with ledger/reports/api/datastore + storefront.' — policy-composition/faithful-floor 08
- Ticket 13, describing docs/shift-left-dev-workflow.md: 'The 2022 bash/Docker checker stays deleted.' — 13
- Ticket 19/25/26 headers: 'the pre-extraction location' language for fleet/pr-gate-check.sh and policy/handbook/generate.sh, both moved to standalone component repos by real-estate epic tickets 03 and 05.
- Ticket 20: 'Confirmed cluster1's own ValidatingPolicy set was byte-for-byte unchanged before and after the run' after cleaning up leaked stray artifacts from an earlier contaminated run — rejecting the earlier state as invalid evidence.
- Ticket 09 quoting ticket 03's Deny gate rationale: 'a candid note that this is the reference demonstrator for the Audit/Deny mechanism split ... not a claim that department labelling meets the gate's catastrophic-risk bar.' — 03
- Ticket 08 major finding: Kyverno 'flattens EVERY installed ValidatingPolicy's objectSelector into ONE shared Kubernetes ValidatingWebhookConfiguration ... the last-reconciled policy silently wins' — rejects the design PRD §6.4 point 3 and CONTEXT.md's 'version self-scoping' specified as actually working; fixed by switching to matchConditions.
- Ticket 10 checklist item: 'The silent-ungovernance gap of the 2022 implementation is demonstrably closed' — explicit supersession of the prior (2022) implementation's behavior.
- Ticket 23, wave-1 correction: 'no such App or scoped token was ever actually set up ... The real guarantee was always the code-level one above, not a permission boundary; SPEC.md and governance-agent/README.md carried the same overclaim and are corrected alongside this.' — rejects an earlier overclaimed enforcement-boundary description.
- Ticket 24, title/description correction: 'the delivered demonstrator deliberately opens a GitHub issue instead' of a PR as ADR-0007's Consequences section had committed to — 'the title/description never caught up to what was actually built and should have.'
- Ticket 17: 'collie's generator/Lula/bootstrap stay dropped' — only the OSCAL catalogue/control-mapping intent is harvested, not collie's tooling.
- Ticket 20: 'No second validation engine' (ADR-0009) paired with independent-schema-validation proof distinct from 'C2P twice'.

### Open items

- Ticket 03: rationale cross-reference fix landed on main but deliberately not backported into a new patch tag for v1.0.3/v2.0.3/v2.2.0 — recorded gap, not planned.
- Ticket 04: v1.0.0 tag left in place broken (tag-flattening CI bug) because tag-protection ruleset blocks moving it and nothing depends on it; v1.0.1 is the first fully green release.
- Ticket 11: live, recurring Renovate GitHub App installation on the org is a standing decision not yet made unilaterally; live-PR proof (vs. dry-run fixture) is a follow-up pending a new tag + that decision.
- Ticket 16: fleet PR #5 for the notification Provider/Alert was open awaiting review at time of the first comment (later merged 2026-07-15); no real chat-webhook credential exists in this environment — generic echo receiver documented as the swap point.
- Ticket 20: independent hardening of c2p-collector's aggregation (checking kustomize.toolkit.fluxcd.io/name label to exclude non-Flux-managed resources) considered and deliberately deferred as unneeded complexity for a demo.
- Ticket 26: CI-wiring of the --with-summaries agent-authored step is a named residual gap, needs an ANTHROPIC_API_KEY secret only the org owner can add.
- Ticket 09 (2026-07-18 follow-up): crossplane-sample Kustomization left permanently Ready:False by design (guard denies Flux's own drift-detection dry-run of the deliberately-unlabeled fixture) — investigated and explicitly left as-is, not fixed.
- Ticket 10: flux-operator crash-looping on cluster1 called out as a still-open, separate question tracked under real-estate epic ticket 16.

**Reviewer notes:** This directory contains only build/implementation tickets (26 of them), not separate decision/ADR records — ADRs (ADR-0001..0009) and the PRD/CONTEXT.md are referenced throughout but live elsewhere in the repo, not under .scratch/faithful-floor/issues/. All 26 tickets show Status: done, but several carry substantial dated follow-up/correction sections added after initial completion (2026-07-17, 2026-07-18, 2026-07-20) from what tickets call 'wave-1' through 'wave-5' skeptic/audit passes — a recurring pattern across this epic of self-reported post-hoc corrections to earlier overclaims. Notable internal corrections/contradictions a reviewer should see: ticket 03 fixed a broken rationale cross-reference in main but deliberately did not re-tag/re-pin the 3 already-pinned versions (v1.0.3/v2.0.3/v2.2.0), calling it a considered, not accidental, gap. Ticket 07 corrected its own claim that CI caught two release incidents — only one actually was, the other found by manual review; also resolved its 'release notes' checklist item via GitHub Release edit rather than a new tag. Ticket 08 is the epic's central technical finding: Kyverno 1.18.2 flattens every installed ValidatingPolicy's objectSelector onto ONE shared ValidatingWebhookConfiguration (last-reconciled wins), silently breaking multi-version coexistence — fixed by switching to matchConditions CEL; ticket 08 also had a stale/superseded paragraph left standing until a wave-3 pass caught it, and a wave-2 audit found its dependsOn/wait verify check had been vacuously passing (wrong label selector, zero matches, loop body never ran) since creation. Ticket 09's 'changes in the same reconcile' claim was false for 3 days (policy-versions.yaml only kubectl-applied, not Flux-reconciled) until fleet#55; same root-cause gap recurred independently on cluster2 (ticket 10, fixed by fleet#62) and was traced by a wave-3 pass to a real, fully-deterministic kustomize-controller implicit-generation race, correcting an earlier 'flakiness' mischaracterization. Ticket 12 had a false 'reviewed, not self-merged' claim corrected by a wave-1 audit (GitHub's own record: zero reviews, self-merged, same as every PR in this solo-dev project). Ticket 19/09 found the orphan guard was structurally blind to unlabeled non-Pod resources (Crossplane CRs) until fleet#60 extended resourceRules. Ticket 20 found its own spike had leaked real writes onto the shared live cluster twice due to an unverified kubectl context assumption, fixed with a hard context guard. Ticket 23's 'never edits enforcement' guarantee was originally overclaimed as a scoped GitHub App token boundary; corrected to a code-level guarantee (no such scoped token was ever actually configured — all writes use full-access personal gh auth). Ticket 13's doc had a stale pinned-version table that silently diverged from reality for 3 days, then the fix itself introduced a v-prefix regression later caught by a wave-5 pass. Tickets 25/26 (handbook generator) were later extracted out of the policy repo into their own component repo by real-estate epic ticket 05, changing the invocation shape — noted in this epic's own corrections. Several tickets (11, 16, 17, 18, 20) record real live GitOps wiring as blocked behind a gitsign OAuth re-authentication step pending the user, later cleared 2026-07-15. Ticket 21 explicitly deviated from the ticket text's named 'github' Grafana datasource in favor of the unauthenticated 'infinity' datasource, disclosed in-panel. Ticket 24's noise-reduction/count claims are dated snapshots explicitly non-reproducible verbatim (the live advisory feed grows over time) — flagged in-ticket as expected variance, not error.

## adrs

**Destination:** Not stated as one sentence in docs/adr/ itself; the ADRs repeatedly invoke 'the thesis' as an external referent. ADR-0001: 'the thesis prizes the git tag as the most legible, reviewable expression of "what is the policy and what version is it."' ADR-0002: 'the thesis's non-negotiable is the reviewed upgrade ("debate happens in pull requests, not exemption requests")'. ADR-0004: 'one versioned-dependency mechanism, one engine, governing both Kubernetes workloads and cloud resources at runtime. Closes the gap the talk admitted.' Recurring destination: policy as a versioned, signed dependency, enforced deterministically by one engine across multiple planes, changed only via reviewed human PRs, measured via layered ground truth including OSCAL attestation.

### Decisions

| Id | Title | Status | Gist | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-0001 | Transport: distribute versioned policy as signed git tags (keyless, gitsign) | accepted | Policy distributed as semver git tags signed keyless with gitsign, consumed via Flux GitRepository pinned on both tag and resolved commit SHA. Chosen over OCI+cosign because OCI's value was mostly working around a current Flux gap; OCI retained as north-star f | docs/adr/0001-transport-signed-git-tags-gitsign.md |
| ADR-0002 | Adoption cadence: pin exact versions everywhere, bump via reviewed Renovate PR | accepted | Every consumer pins an exact policy tag+commit SHA; new versions adopted only via reviewed, automerge:false Renovate PR everywhere. Rejects Flux live semver ranges as deleting the review gate. | docs/adr/0002-adoption-pinned-plus-renovate-pr.md |
| ADR-0003 | Engine API: Kyverno CEL ValidatingPolicy, not the 2022 ClusterPolicy | accepted | Policies authored as Kyverno ValidatingPolicy (CEL) not deprecated ClusterPolicy. Multi-version coexistence corrected to matchConditions after live testing found objectSelector flattens across policies onto one shared webhook (last-reconciled wins). | docs/adr/0003-kyverno-validatingpolicy-cel.md |
| ADR-0004 | Cloud/IaC plane: harvest ControlPlane's collie (cloud-as-CR), not Checkov | accepted | Cloud plane built by harvesting collie's OSCAL catalogue and policy intent as data; hand-authored CEL ValidatingPolicy governs Crossplane CRs via the same Kyverno engine. collie's generator, Lula wiring, bootstrap harness dropped. | docs/adr/0004-cloud-plane-fork-collie.md |
| ADR-0005 | Install & fleet layer: ControlPlane Flux Operator (FluxInstance + ResourceSet) | accepted | Flux installed/managed via ControlPlane's Flux Operator instead of vanilla flux bootstrap; cluster x version coexistence matrix expressed as a ResourceSet. Guardrail: thesis must remain vanilla-Flux-expressible. | docs/adr/0005-controlplane-flux-operator-resourceset.md |
| ADR-0006 | Policy code is deterministic — no time-conditional state | accepted | No expiry/date-based CEL logic in policy bodies. 'Delete-if-undefended' realised as editorial PR, not mechanical date trigger; review metadata is advisory-only. | docs/adr/0006-deterministic-policy-no-time-conditions.md |
| ADR-0007 | Governance: agent-assisted editorial review (the human layer the mea-culpa deman | accepted | Human governance realised as editorial review supported by an AI/agent layer that surfaces business decisions as issues/PRs but never edits enforcement. Two in-text self-corrections retract earlier claims: demonstrator opens GitHub issues not PRs; no scoped Gi | docs/adr/0007-agent-assisted-editorial-governance.md |
| ADR-0008 | "Measurable" = layered ground-truth; PR-acceptance demoted to adoption velocity | accepted | Measurable pillar = four panels over four datasources (Flux revision, PolicyReports, OSCAL via C2P, Renovate PR state), not one PromQL join. PR-acceptance relabelled adoption velocity, not compliance proof. Phased: P1=revision+PolicyReports, OSCAL lands P2. | docs/adr/0008-measurable-layered-ground-truth.md |
| ADR-0009 | OSCAL attestation via Compliance-to-Policy (C2P), not Lula | accepted | OSCAL assessment-results produced by C2P's result2oscal from Kyverno PolicyReports; Lula dropped entirely (Lula 2 dropped OSCAL, lula1 unmaintained/redundant). Live KiND spike (2026-07-14) proved the mapping with a small jq shim. | docs/adr/0009-oscal-attestation-via-c2p.md |
| ADR-0010 | Sunset: scheduled proposals, never scheduled application | accepted | A fleet array entry may carry a sunset date; on the date a machine opens a retirement PR that a human must merge. Enforced by allow_auto_merge:false and require-pr-gate. Extends ADR-0006's boundary: timed reminders ok, timed enforcement not. | docs/adr/0010-sunset-scheduled-proposals-not-application.md |
| ADR-0011 | Release gate: compute the bump, refuse a weaker declaration, no override | accepted | Publisher and adopter gates compute semver bump from observed verdict movement and refuse a weaker declared bump; never rewrite it. No override at any scope — would be the banned exemptions ledger by another name. | docs/adr/0011-release-gate-computes-the-bump.md |
| ADR-0012 | Composed artefact: self-signed by the adopter, reproducible from pinned parent S | accepted | Composed policy set self-signed by the adopter using the same gitsign tag mechanism; parent digest is the already-pinned resolved commit SHA, not a new content hash. | docs/adr/0012-composed-artefact-self-signed-pinned-sha.md |
| ADR-0013 | The regulator publishes named baselines, the adopter selects one, and both key o | accepted | Regulator publishes named signed OSCAL baselines; adopter selects one, may add controls but never remove one. Exact-string bare id resolution. Estate selects MODERATE (285 recorded holes day one). New hole refuses composition; pre-existing hole recorded. | docs/adr/0013-regulator-publishes-baselines-adopter-selects.md |
| ADR-0014 | An unclaimed pod is caged, not denied; a governed namespace requires a claim at  | accepted | Resolves contradiction: CONTEXT.md claimed the orphan guard denies unlabelled pods; committed guard actually skips them (correctly, or it would brick kube-system/Flux/Kyverno and break de-posture UPDATEs). Real hole closed by a new CREATE-only ValidatingPolicy | docs/adr/0014-unclaimed-is-caged-governed-namespace-requires-claim.md |
| ADR-0015 | The proposer: the adopter runs it, in its own repo, and it opens the PR | accepted | Existing war-gamer becomes the tier-drift proposer; adopter runs it in its own repo on its own token and it now commits/pushes/opens the PR (previously stopped at the diff). No schedule exists anywhere — named blind spot for EOL drift. Deny-tier coercion-to-ba | docs/adr/0015-adopter-runs-the-proposer-and-it-opens-the-pr.md |
| ADR-0016 | A subclass never restates a mutate: the tier is the only knob, and only the prop | accepted | Restatement/strictness comparison applies only to ValidatingPolicy members; Mutating/GeneratingPolicy have no ladder so restating one is refused. Fixes a spike bug (validationActions written onto every member kind) and a resolver-key collision (silent overwrit | docs/adr/0016-a-subclass-never-restates-a-mutate.md |
| ADR-0017 | A control claim belongs to whoever ships the implementation, and an adopter's ow | accepted | A control is filled by a signed OSCAL claim from whoever ships the implementation (any party). An adopter-added control with no implementation is an ordinary new hole; adopter may never claim against another party's policy or remove a control it added. | docs/adr/0017-a-control-claim-belongs-to-whoever-ships-the-implementat |
| ADR-0018 | The Namespace manifest is the governed declaration; the composed artefact carrie | accepted | The governed:"true" label on each adopter's own signed Namespace manifest is the sole governed-scope declaration; composed artefact carries it only as advisory, re-derivable metadata. Composition refuses a new ungoverned namespace, records a pre-existing one. | docs/adr/0018-the-namespace-manifest-is-the-governed-declaration.md |

### Rejections and supersessions

- ADR-0001: OCI+cosign 'Rejected as complexity introduced to dodge a Flux gap, not to serve the thesis. Retained as the north-star option.'
- ADR-0001: 'Git tags + long-lived GPG keys (rejected)... reintroduces GPG key custody across a fleet.'
- ADR-0002: Flux live semver ranges rejected: 'silently delete that review gate.' Per-environment/per-policy-class splits 'Recorded as a north-star option only.'
- ADR-0003: ClusterPolicy rejected as a 'dying API'; ValidatingAdmissionPolicy 'rejected for the floor — it drops PolicyReports/mutation/generation and abandons the reference engine.'
- ADR-0004: 'We do not inherit collie's generator, Lula wiring, or bootstrap.' collie's policies called 'a generated, static demo.'
- ADR-0004/0009: 'defenseunicorns/lula is now Lula 2 — a SvelteKit/Express compliance-documentation web app... that deliberately dropped OSCAL... Lula is dropped entirely.'
- ADR-0005: vanilla flux bootstrap rejected for the floor but 'retained as the documented fallback per the guardrail.'
- ADR-0006: 'This supersedes the rejected expiry options (engine hard-expiry; class-aware auto-removal on a date)... none of them ship.'
- ADR-0007: 'this originally said "opens PRs" -- the actual demonstrator... deliberately opens GitHub issues instead.' Second correction: 'the first correction's own wording... repeated the same overclaim... no scoped GitHub App or token was ever set up.'
- ADR-0008: 'The headline shifts from the 2022 pitch ("compliance is a GitHub PR search away") to demonstrable control satisfaction with evidence... a deliberate departure from the talk's framing.'
- ADR-0011: 'Trust the declared bump, test it after the fact (rejected — status quo)... That is post-hoc justification, not derivation.' 'An override on this gate is the same shape wearing a different label... That is the exemptions ledger this estate already deleted.'
- ADR-0013: 'Refuse on any hole. Rejected: 285 holes on day one would deny the whole estate for ever. That is a wall, not a gate.'
- ADR-0013: 'The cs-06b prototype's case-folding check is void... it must be rewritten.' 'The cs-06b prototype modelled it as a hypothetical nist v2.0.0 addition, and that modelling is superseded.'
- ADR-0014: 'CONTEXT.md as written. Rejected on the facts above.' 'The locked-door claim is narrowed... The gate tier is an opt-in door, not the locked door CONTEXT.md claimed.'
- ADR-0014: 'An exemption list for infrastructure namespaces. Rejected on the map's standing preference. Exemptions are banned.'
- ADR-0015: 'platform runs it and opens PRs across orgs (Rejected)... no scoped app or token was ever set up. The estate should not invent one for this.'
- ADR-0016: 'The spike had this defect and it is fixed... render() wrote spec.validationActions onto every member unconditionally... It is now guarded by kind.'
- ADR-0016: 'The old resolver key overwrites in silence... That is luck, not design. A named gap in the spike, closed in the new loader.'
- ADR-0016: 'Let the overlay declare a per-party tier floor... Rejected. A floor in the overlay is a verdict set by a declaration rather than by the £. That is the override this model does not have.'
- ADR-0018: 'The composed artefact lists namespaces and the label is rendered from it (Rejected)... restates a signed fact in a second place.'

### Open items

- ADR-0001: on-cluster Flux-native gitsign gate blocked on fluxcd/source-controller#1068, tracked not blocking.
- ADR-0001: signed attestations of the 'why' and SBOM deferred to the OCI north-star report.
- ADR-0004: UK catalogue (NCSC CAF/GovAssure) can be added later, currently illustrative US NIST framing.
- ADR-0007: non-technical human adoption of the handbook 'remains partly cultural and is named as a residual open problem'; the last-mile handbook itself is marked 'proposed — confirm'.
- ADR-0009: C2P is pre-GA (v2.0.0-rc.1), pinned/vendored, 'track for GA.'
- ADR-0013: MODERATE baseline starts at 285 recorded holes to be individually implemented or accepted.
- ADR-0014: platform must still build the specified CREATE-only governed-namespace ValidatingPolicy; every governed namespace must be labelled before Deny.
- ADR-0015: no recurring schedule exists for the tier proposer — EOL-feed drift 'a named blind spot, not a closed one... the estate has declined that standing decision on purpose.' Deny-coercion bug recorded as real platform defect. proposer_bounds.py's ponytail upgrade path 'stays unbuilt.'
- ADR-0016: 'No new refusal reaches an adopter today... The rule is written before the first case, not after it.' Composition proven faithful but 'does not prove the twin matches what flux-operator renders in-cluster.'
- ADR-0018: three adopter Namespace manifests (driftwood, tuppence, ludlow) still need the governed:"true" label. Cluster label drift is untracked at merge time: 'Nothing at merge time can see a namespace created by hand.'

**Reviewer notes:** All 18 files carry 'status: accepted' with no other lifecycle states present. ADR-0007 uniquely contains two dated in-text self-corrections (2026-07-18, wave-1 and wave-2 audit) retracting its own earlier overclaims about the governance agent's write scope. ADR-0009 through ADR-0018 form a connected reasoning chain, each frequently correcting or narrowing an earlier ADR or CONTEXT.md (e.g. 0014 narrows CONTEXT.md's 'locked door' framing; 0016 fixes a spike bug discovered while writing the ADR). Several ADRs (0011,0013,0014,0015,0016,0018) describe defects found and fixed in the same commit as the ADR itself, so they read as combined decision+fix records. ADR-0013/14/15/16/17/18 reference an outside 'map' and numbered tickets (01, 03, 04, 13) living outside docs/adr/ that were not read as part of this group. No formal 'tickets' with id/title/status/acceptance/verify-command exist inside docs/adr/ itself; the closest is ADR-0009's build-precondition spike with a runnable path (spikes/c2p-validatingpolicy-oscal/), captured under claims_built rather than as a ticket.

## docs-core

**Destination:** "Re-implement CNS's Policy as [Versioned] Code thesis on Flux CD — distributing organisational policy as a semantically-versioned, signed dependency, governed by a single Kyverno engine across both a Kubernetes workload plane and a Crossplane cloud plane, with reviewed (PR-gated) upgrades, multi-version runtime coexistence, ground-truth compliance, and an agent-assisted human-governance layer — proven reproducibly on KiND." (docs/PRD.md, one-line summary). PRD posture: "Faithful-to-intent build. A separate north-star report documents the fuller modern reference." README frames PRD.md as "the faithful-to-intent build, phased P1→P2→P3" and north-star-modern-reference.md as "the fuller modern reference the floor grows into."

### Decisions

| Id | Title | Status | Gist | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ADR-0001 | Transport: signed git tags (gitsign, keyless), commit-SHA pinning | Referenced from PRD as binding; ADR file itself not in this  | Policy released as semver git tags signed keyless via gitsign (Sigstore/Fulcio/Rekor). Flux GitRepository pins spec.ref.tag AND spec.ref.commit. Known limitation accepted: Flux GitRepository.spec.verify is PGP-only, cannot verify gitsign — no Flux-native verif | docs/PRD.md §6.1, §3, §10 |
| ADR-0002 | Adoption: pinned everywhere + Renovate PR; two axes independent | Referenced from PRD as binding; ADR file not in this group's | Exact tags+commit pinned everywhere; new versions land only via reviewed CI-gated Renovate PR (automerge:false). Adoption cadence is independent of enforcement action. A single Renovate customManager (git-refs datasource) bumps every {tag,commit} pin, since Re | docs/PRD.md §3, §6.2 |
| ADR-0003 | Engine & policy authoring: Kyverno CEL ValidatingPolicy | Referenced from PRD as binding; ADR file not in this group's | Policies are CEL ValidatingPolicy; validationActions is the enforcement axis (Audit=lane-keeping, Deny=gate), independent of adoption cadence; requires Kyverno >=1.18; the engine's HelmRelease is itself pinned and bumped via the same reviewed Renovate path. | docs/PRD.md §6.3 |
| ADR-0004 | Cloud plane: harvest collie, rebuild native | Referenced from PRD as binding; ADR file not in this group's | Harvest controlplaneio/collie's IP (NIST 800-53r5 policy intent + OSCAL catalogue) rather than fork its toolchain (legacy ClusterPolicy generated via now-dropped Lula 1); hand-author CEL policies against current Crossplane v2 + AWS provider CRDs; collie's gene | docs/PRD.md §6.5; docs/references.md; README.md |
| ADR-0005 | Multi-version coexistence: ResourceSet matrix; Operator is sugar not mechanism | Referenced from PRD as binding; ADR file not in this group's | N GitRepository objects + nameSuffix collision-free objects + matchConditions CEL version self-scoping (not matchConstraints objectSelector) + workload opt-in label + fleet-held single {version,commit} array ranged over by ResourceSet templates. "The Operator  | docs/PRD.md §3, §6.4 |
| ADR-0006 | Deterministic policy; editorial (not automated/timed) review and retirement | Referenced from PRD as binding; ADR file not in this group's | No time-conditional logic in policy bodies. Dated/reviewed/removed-if-undefended is an editorial PR action — never automated or timed; orphan guard may start in Audit and promote to Deny only by editorial PR. | docs/PRD.md §3, §6.4 point 7, §6.7 |

### Rejections and supersessions

- "A gate-only system is the exact mistake the mea-culpa walked back." — docs/PRD.md §1
- "Shortfalls this PRD closes or honestly carries: the bespoke bash/Docker checker and its bugs (hardcoded URL, no caching, hcl2json mismatch, Checkov drift), signing the checker image rather than the policy, the cloud/Terraform side being shift-left-only (admitted on stage), the orphaned-version-label silent gap, compliance measured as PR-acceptance rather than ground truth, and the absence of any governance or last-mile story." — docs/PRD.md §1.1
- "No bespoke tooling — native CLIs for shift-left; the bash checker is deleted, not ported" — docs/PRD.md §3
- "collie's generator, Lula wiring, and EKS/Terraform bootstrap are dropped, not ported." — docs/PRD.md §6.5
- "this is why Lula was dropped: it ran its own parallel checks" — docs/PRD.md §6.6
- "We harvest controlplaneio/collie (Apache-2.0) rather than fork its toolchain." — docs/PRD.md §6.5
- "matchConditions CEL expression -- not matchConstraints objectSelector, which Kyverno's admission-controller flattens across every installed ValidatingPolicy into one shared webhook ... silently breaking coexistence" — docs/PRD.md §6.4 point 3
- "A customManager is not bespoke tooling in the sense the 'no bespoke tooling' principle forbids ...; the exemption is explicit." — docs/PRD.md §6.2
- "this top-level repo (policy-as-versioned-flux ...) is superseded and becomes research-only" — docs/ARCHIVE.md
- "No further feature work lands here." — docs/ARCHIVE.md
- "The floor rejects live ranges for policy (ADR-0002) and the north-star does not reverse this — reviewed upgrades are thesis-core." — docs/north-star-modern-reference.md §10
- "an earlier version of this sentence claimed each of the two files calls all three commands — release.yml has no flux build" — docs/shift-left-dev-workflow.md
- "an earlier version of this section hardcoded a version/tag/commit table 'as of this writing' — it drifted the very next day" — docs/shift-left-dev-workflow.md §0

## research-original

**Destination:** From 03: "Treat policy as a versioned software dependency, not as a gate." Quote: "If policy is a dependency, then it should behave like one. It should have a version number. It should follow semantic versioning. It should live in a Git repository." Refined (mea culpa) position: "Policy should be lane keeping assist where lane keeping assist is appropriate... Policy should be a dependency — versioned, tested, consumed, and updated automatically — not a gate" but "Some policies belong at the gate... I want a locked door." From 20 (destination of this research group): "This document is Phase 1 — faithful expansion only... to expand CNS's 'Policy as [Versioned] Code' onto Flux primitives 1:1, NOT to redesign it." From 21: the companion "principal-engineer subtraction pass CNS asked for: 'research how to modernise (or better yet consider where it could be SIMPLIFIED).'" From 22: "A single de-duplicated, prioritised register of every open design decision raised by the faithful-mapping (20) and simplify/modernise (21) syntheses. Drives the requirements-grilling session before the PRD."

Register flags this as the sharpest disagreement to grill. | research/22-prd-decision-register.md |

### Rejections and supersessions

- "Some policies belong at the gate." — the mea culpa (quoted in 03) rejects the original all-dependency framing: "The original 'policy as dependency' framing over-reached." (research/03-blogs-thesis.md §4c)
- "A gate-only system is the exact mistake the mea-culpa walked back." (research/21-synthesis-simplify-modernise.md §8)
- The hcl2tojson binary-name mismatch, checkov version drift (2.1.242 vs 3.2.485), and no-auth-for-private-policy bugs in policy-checker are explicitly said to not be inherited by the port (research/21 §1.1, research/01 §2.5, research/02 §3.1)
- "Do NOT swap Renovate for Flux image-automation here — image-automation is commit-first with a clunky, forge-specific PR story ... which loses the PR-debate ethos CNS cares about." (research/21-synthesis-simplify-modernise.md §1.4)
- "NOT faithful — surrenders the per-version human review gate the original deliberately kept" — rejects pure live-range resolution as default for policy (research/20-synthesis-faithful-flux-mapping.md §1.4)
- "policy-checker ... DELETE" — "the single biggest, safest subtraction" (research/21-synthesis-simplify-modernise.md §1.1)
- "policy-action (reusable workflow) → DELETE entirely. Already deprecated in the original" (research/21 §1.2)
- "'Count open PRs' compliance proxy" rejected as "a GitHub-org-shaped proxy" measuring bump-acceptance not compliance (research/21 §1.5)
- "the git-tag origin was a 2022 artefact, not a design choice" — rejects treating original transport as intentional (research/22 D1.1)
- Checkov's BATS test harness and continue-on-error CI called out as a deliberate hack in the original, not a design to preserve (research/01 §1.6-1.7, research/21 §5 ledger #10)
- "ClusterPolicy (Kyverno v1) ... Migrate. ClusterPolicy deprecated, removal ~1.20" (research/21 §7 ledger #8) — supersedes the original's exclusive legacy ClusterPolicy usage
- "Weave GitOps OSS ... effectively broken; ... Treat as legacy; prefer Capacitor." (research/15-flux-notifications-compliance.md §8)

### Open items

- All decision-register items (D1.1 through E2, 20 items) are explicitly unresolved pending a 'requirements-grilling session' with CNS before the PRD is written (research/22).
- 20's and 21's 'OPEN QUESTIONS FOR CNS' sections (Q1-Q10 each) restate/expand the same unresolved trade-offs — none answered within this document set.
- The 'last mile' to non-technical consumers is flagged to stay 'an explicit open problem in the PRD rather than claiming Flux solves it' (research/03 §7 item 6).
- Human-governance layer (dated/reviewed/delete-if-undefended) is thesis-core but Flux gives nothing for it; register D8.2 recommends parking it as future work.
- The 'orphaned version label' silent-un-guarding risk (original's cluster2 dropping v1.0.0) was never closed by the original and is only a proposed, undecided mitigation here (research/02 §1.6, research/20 Q5, research/22 D2.2).
- Terraform/cloud runtime-lifecycle gap: Checkov-in-CI has no runtime loop; a real GitOps answer exists (tofu-controller/Crossplane) but adoption cost/scope undecided (D5.2).

**Reviewer notes:** This group contains NO decision tickets, ADRs, or build/implementation tickets in the tracker sense — research/22 is itself an OPEN register of design questions awaiting a grilling session, not a record of decisions taken; every 'decision' above is unresolved as of this document set, recommendation-only (default + confidence). research/22 explicitly flags D1.2 (pin vs range) as 'the sharpest cross-doc disagreement' between its own source syntheses 20 and 21 — a real internal contradiction, not just an open question. research/01 and research/02 document TWO DIFFERENT prior-art GitHub orgs with overlapping but distinct version lines (example-policy-org: 1.0.0/2.0.0/2.0.1; policy-as-versioned-code: 1.0.0/2.0.0/2.1.0/2.1.1) — the two syntheses (20/21) treat policy-as-versioned-code's cluster1/cluster2 as the canonical crux example; readers should not conflate 'the original' between the two orgs. research/03 explicitly warns not to invent meaning for the mea-culpa slug 'pedef.' Files 10-17 are pure Flux technical reference (dated June 2026) with no CNS decisions or claims of their own — pure footnoted source material for 20/21/22. Numbering gaps (04-09, 18-19 absent) are consistent with a phased structure, not missing files. Overall: a later drift review should treat essentially everything in this group as 'researched and recommended, awaiting CNS confirmation' — any downstream ticket/PRD asserting these as settled decisions would be overclaiming relative to this source.

## talk-spec-research

**Destination:** Each file is background research feeding a conference-talk build spec, resolving a numbered issue ticket and informing specific downstream design tickets. 07: "give whoever builds the risk model (04) and the balance-sheet view (06) enough to implement a lightweight, defensible FAIR-style engine ... so that 'Audit → Deny' is a number, not a vibe." 08: informs "the architecture decision (02), the exemptions ledger (05), and the shift-left check (03)" under the frame "Flux CD is fixed and load-bearing ... Everything else ... is judged on merit." 09: "does OSCAL natively carry the RISK half? (accepted / residual / priced) ... Feeds exemptions-ledger (05) and balance-sheet (06)." 16: "can Kyverno's admission-time policy posture ... become a real, attested property of the workload's SPIRE SVID, so that Istio AuthorizationPolicy and OpenBao gate on it?" 18: "give humans phishing-resistant, device-trusted, per-session-RBAC, audited operational access (kubectl / dashboards / break-glass) to a KinD-on-a-laptop estate that already runs SPIFFE/SPIRE ... gitsign keyless ... Flux, Kyverno."

### Rejections and supersessions

- 07: 'You cannot just multiply the means: risk is skewed and fat-tailed, and the board cares about the tail, not the average.' (implicit rejection of point-estimate risk framing)
- 08: 'Rejected as the floor by ADR-0003 (drops PolicyReports, mutation, generation, the offline CLI)' — re native ValidatingAdmissionPolicy without Kyverno.
- 08 on jsPolicy: 'effectively dormant ... Betting a 2026 governance reference on it contradicts "faithful to intent". Excluded.'
- 08 on Crossplane modelling the version contract: 'Not needed; the ResourceSet inputs array already IS that contract ... Skip it (ponytail: speculative abstraction ...)'
- 08: 'Switching engines buys nothing when Flux, not the engine, is the fixed anchor — and costs a language migration + rewrite of the working shift-left path.'
- 18: 'Teleport OSS is the wrong pick for an "open-source, demonstrably attested device trust" story — and it fails on a second axis too.'
- 18: 'do not tell the audience "Teleport is Apache-2.0 open source" — it isn't anymore' (Community-edition license restriction since v16).
- 18: 'HashiCorp Boundary is BSL 1.1 (not OSI-OSS since Aug 2023)' — excluded from the OSS-only recommendation on that basis.
- 18: Cloudflare/StrongDM/Twingate/Google BeyondCorp all rejected as 'proprietary and/or SaaS' / 'not self-hostable on a laptop'.
- 16: 'Custom claims per registration entry are not natively supported by SPIRE ... Putting posture in the path sidesteps that entirely and is the recommended route.'

- 08: 'enablePolicyException + exceptionNamespace must be turned on and scoped before 05 can ship' — not done as of this doc.
- 08: exception expiry mechanism choice (ledger+Flux-prune vs cleanup.kyverno.io/ttl) flagged 'decide in 02' — undecided.
- 08: whether to adopt Warn as a distinct middle tier, and whether to add parameterised numeric thresholds, flagged 'decide in 02' / build-only-if-needed.
- 09: exemptions ledger (05) must become a generator of risk objects — not yet implemented; C2P output stops at findings today.
- 16: a 'currency controller' to re-patch/remove stale posture labels is explicitly not built.
- 16: Kyverno validate rule blocking user-supplied posture.* labels described as required but not confirmed implemented.
- 18: TPM availability for the presenting laptop unresolved (Apple Silicon needs a Linux VM with virtual TPM).
- 18: whether Pomerium Core exposes WebAuthn attestation=direct + FIDO MDS verification flagged as open, not confirmed.
- 18: if both device-attestation-over-OIDC and session recording are required, running two OSS components side by side is not decided/committed.

**Reviewer notes:** ["None of these five files contain a formal decision-ticket/ADR structure with an owner bare-agree pattern — they are research memos with at most a one-line Status/ticket-resolution header. The decisions array is intentionally empty; the tickets array is the closest analogue.", "07 and 09 are explicit that their outputs feed not-yet-built downstream tickets (04, 05, 06); 08 is the only one grounded in and citing live repo state, and it reports one real bug found-and-fixed live during the research.", "16 and 18 lack the 'Status: resolved · Ticket: ...' header the other three use; 18 substitutes 'All web facts verified 2026-07-31' as its freshness claim.", "18's licensing-trap findings (Teleport Community no longer Apache-2.0 since v16; Boundary BSL 1.1) directly reverse a naive 'just use Teleport, it's open source' assumption and are load-bearing for any downstream claim.", "07's implementation section opens with 'Ladder check: don't build a CRQ platform' — a deliberate scope-limiting frame; several sub-parts of the FAIR taxonomy are explicitly folded/skipped rather than fully modelled."]

## real-estate

**Destination:** "Make the estate real and let the existing mechanism prove itself against it. Five real app teams in five repos, with real stacks, deliberately mixed dependency hygiene, and one team owning the cloud-plane claims. Extract every reusable component into its own versioned, pinned repo... Close the gate's version cross-check hole. Give the CIO the forward-looking answers... Wire the live Renovate service in behind an org-level preset. Add follow-through tracking to the governance checkboxes without ever touching policy content. One new dashboard joins the two kinds of staleness — policy versions and app dependencies — into a single per-team estate view, which *strengthens* the core metaphor: a policy version is just another dependency." (spec.md). Born from show+tell demo feedback (2026-07-16) that exposed the prior faithful-floor epic's estate as "a cardboard cutout" (spec.md, Problem Statement).

### Rejections and supersessions

- "the demo exposed that the estate around the mechanism is a cardboard cutout. The consumer apps are three identical nginx pods in one monorepo" — spec.md, Problem Statement
- "the cloud resources are planted by the platform rather than requested by a team" — spec.md, Problem Statement
- "reusable machinery (the PR gate, the OSCAL collector, the handbook generator) is buried inside the repos it happens to have grown in" — spec.md, Problem Statement
- "Renovate — the entire 'updatable' story — only ever ran against local fixtures until the user installed the real app mid-demo" — spec.md, Problem Statement
- "No history rewrite, ever, in place: the endgame for 'clean history' is a from-scratch redeploy into a fresh org when the epic completes... with the current org kept as the audit trail." — spec.md
- "No SBOM." (rejected at grilling as hollow for YAML) — spec.md and issue 14
- ADR-0010 "extends ADR-0006's boundary deliberately" — disclosed extension, not silent — issue 02
- "Two-org separation (components org / model org) is deferred and recorded" — spec.md and issue 15
- "the OSCAL collection job becomes a component repo... replacing the build-C2P-from-source-every-run CronJob (the ticket-21 tradeoff, now retired)" — issue 04, supersedes a prior faithful-floor tradeoff
- "the real numbers overturn the 'worst-in-class on both axes' framing rather than confirming it: ledger has 22 vulnerabilities, reports has 188, storefront has 146 — ledger is the least vulnerable of the three... Left as an honest, interesting finding rather than reshaped to fit the original thesis." — issue 12
- "'Zero lines needed' is corrected to 'zero new lines beyond a minimal local file Mend requires regardless of org config.'" — issue 06
- issue 16: two in-repo mitigations for the ResourceSet flap explicitly REJECTED — "speculative without release-note access" and "a much larger architectural change that would undermine ADR-0005's whole rationale"

### Open items

- Ticket 09: the retirement-PR branch of the sunset-escalator cron is fixed and proven up to the last step, but blocked on a single org-level GitHub setting ('Allow GitHub Actions to create and approve pull requests', OFF org-wide) that needs an org admin (admin:org scope) to flip — explicitly not closed as of the ticket's last write-up (2026-07-20).
- Ticket 16 (not a build ticket): flux-operator's ResourceSet controller flap is documented, not fixed; no in-repo mitigation taken; flagged as a candidate for a future session or an upstream bug report.
- Spec Out of Scope items, all deferred: two-org separation; the fresh-org redeploy itself (the epic's sequel); any history rewrite or repo deletion; SBOM generation; automatic application of anything timed; cross-cluster Prometheus (Thanos/federation); real cloud credentials.
- Ticket 16's flap-frequency baseline (~24 times over 4 days) flagged as possibly non-representative, measured under an unusually heavy adversarial-verification load.
- Ticket 11's scanning-stack operational flakiness (probe failures/restarts) noted as an ongoing live caveat, not something fixed.

**Reviewer notes:** This is a heavily self-auditing document set: nearly every ticket (01, 04, 06, 07, 08, 09, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15, 16) carries one or more dated 'Follow-up'/'Correction' sections, several explicitly attributed to 'adversarial audit'/'skeptic'/'wave-N' passes that found real, previously-unflagged gaps in the ticket's own original claims — including at least one case (ticket 13) where a checked acceptance-criteria box had literally no code behind it, and one case (ticket 08) where a checked claim was live-false at the time it was written. Ticket 09 is the single ticket whose top-line 'Status: done' is directly undercut by its own final follow-up (2026-07-20), which states the retirement-PR mechanism is not actually fully closed pending an org-admin toggle — the ticket file itself anticipates its own status may be stale. Ticket 16 is not a build ticket but a discovered-and-recorded known issue (status 'documented, not fixed') sitting in the issues directory alongside the 15 build/decision tickets; its own root-cause narrative was revised twice in place by later adversarial passes. Multiple tickets record bugs caught only by actually running things live rather than trusting docs/local tests — a recurring epistemic pattern (ticket 01's yq di==0 bug, ticket 04's Flux-managed-directory silent-revert, ticket 10's kind:List silent-zero-output and busybox ash crash, ticket 11's chain of scanner timeout/mode bugs, one of which was an explicitly WRONG initial root-cause theory). Vulnerability and team counts shift across tickets as real data landed, in ticket 12's case directly contradicting the original design-intent narrative rather than confirming it.

## talk-spec-decisions

**Destination:** map.md Destination: "A **conference-grade talk** (first-class **Marp** deck) that lands one thesis — *governance should be a **proportionate, informed response to quantified risk**, and versioning the whole chain from risk-appetite to evidence is how you keep that proportionality honest, current, and provable* — **backed by a fully-real, refactored demo estate** where every claim is demo-live, not narrated. ... Done when: the talk spec (spine, claims, deck) is locked **and** the estate is refactored so nothing in the talk is faked or hinted." the-whole-model.md Thesis: "Governance is a **proportionate, informed, continuously re-tuned response to quantified risk** — and versioning the whole chain from risk-appetite to evidence, with **every actor attestable**, is how proportionality stays honest and current as the world moves." Tightest form: "policy is a versioned dependency — and that one dependency is the whole governance surface." spec.md Solution: a "~35–40 min, touring" Marp deck plus "the **delivery refactor**: a fresh six-org estate (`policy-as-versioned-{platform,driftwood,tuppence,ludlow,nist,ico}`), one KinD cluster per institution, where every claim is demo-live rather than narrated."

**Reviewer notes:** 1) Ticket-count / status disagreement: 19 issue files exist; 18 are Status: resolved and one (12) is Status: claimed. map.md's 'Decisions so far' index lists only 16 of them — it omits 03 (shift-left) and 12 (Renovate); 17 and 19 appear only as parenthetical references inside the 16 and 18 entries. The index comment says 'closed tickets', but 12 is not closed and is silently absent.
2) map.md 'Out of scope' says '(none ruled out yet)' while spec.md carries a seven-item Out of Scope list (migration, dev cluster, live balance-sheet beats, Crossplane version contract, narrated-vision productionisation, FAIR sophistication, Teleport, real-TPM/MDM). The map is stale against the spec here.
3) Ticket 16's blockquote 'Reopens the map (was decision-complete). Pauses publishing the Phase 0–5 build tickets' and ticket 18's 'Reopens the map; build-ticket publish stays paused' are both still in the ticket bodies even though map.md now declares 'All design decisions locked; spec folded; build tickets published' with 27 slices. Stale banners.
4) Ticket 18 contains a mid-file 'Awaiting: human confirm + the Mac/TPM fidelity call' and an 'Open: (a) add one real TPM machine … or (b) narrate the TPM tier' block that the later Answer supersedes (UTM vTPM VMs, no extra hardware) without striking through — a reader could take the Open block as live.
5) Phase-count drift: map.md's build fog says 'Phases 0–5' in one place while its own preceding paragraph and both spec.md and the-whole-model.md say Phases 0–7. Ticket 16 also refers to 'the unpublished Phase 0–5 build tickets'.
6) Nothing in this group is a build ticket with tests/commands. The only two items with execution evidence are 12 (partial, no PR observed) and 15 (rename, no captured command output). Every other resolution ends with 'is build-work, not a remaining decision'. This group records DECISIONS almost entirely; claims_built is thin — research artifacts, six empty orgs, and the org rename.
7) The one claim of running code is the '~40-line numpy fair.py reference already drafted + self-checked' — asserted in issues/07 and map.md with no path inside the read set other than research/07-risk-quantification.md. Worth verifying against what actually exists.
8) 'ico' penalties are described as 'real public fine magnitudes, repackaged' and 'never fabricated rulings' (01) — an honesty guardrail a drift review should check the built artifact against.
9) The driftwood-vs-ludlow money shot is stated with a specific multiple, '~8x', in issues/01 but nowhere else; issues/04 and spec.md say the exact £ derive during build. If a built artifact hard-codes 8x, it came from this ticket, not from FAIR.
10) issues/06 has TWO answers: the original '## Answer' (residual → balance sheet) and a later '## Update (2026-07-23) — the number is Total Cost of Risk, not just residual'. A reader stopping at the first Answer gets the superseded definition.
11) The 'demonstrable-core vs narrated-vision' split is the group's central honesty device and is stated consistently three times (map, the-whole-model, spec): live = proportionality, living loop, provenance; narrated = breach-cost open, balance-sheet close, industry-norm regulator publishing, insurance/board consumption, PQ scenarios, Windows/Linux EUD virtualisation.
12) spec.md status is 'ready-for-agent' with no date; the fold-in date at the foot is 2026-07-31, the latest date anywhere in this group. Everything here predates the pitch-v4/v6 and twin work referenced elsewhere in the repo — treat this group as the ORIGINAL intent snapshot, not current state.

## talk-spec-pitches

**Destination:** plan.md spine: "An organisation only ever asks one question — what should we do next, and what does it cost if we are wrong? This is what happens when you build the machine that answers it, make versioned governance its enactment arm, and then refuse to lie about either." RUNBOOK.md: "governance is a proportionate, informed, continuously re-tuned response to quantified risk — and versioning the whole chain, with every actor attestable, is how proportionality stays honest." deck.md title: "Governance is a priced, versioned judgement... It's a priced, versioned, continuously re-tuned judgement."

## twin-research

**Destination:** This is a research corpus (not a decision/build tracker) feeding the org digital-twin project: modelling an organisation as a versioned Wardley/knowledge graph, sensing weak signals, pricing risk and opportunity in a single currency, and validating the whole thing against real-world backtests. Per twin-wardley-horizon-sota.md: "The twin = the graph we already have... a versioned knowledge graph of the estate, projected as Wardley maps, nudged by STEEP-classified weak signals that propagate along dependency edges, and replayed / fast-forwarded through git history for scenario what-if." Per blind-spots-fable.md, the underlying worry driving several of these research tracks: "every loop in this system — validation, calibration, falsification, the quality yardstick itself — currently closes through the same single mind. A twin whose ground truth, detector, scenarios, evaluation, and standard of success all come from one builder is not a falsifiable system with a synthetic substrate; it is a mirror with extra steps."

- "Proposal A — compare aggregate Brier scores. Reject." and "Proposal B — score the twin against market prices as the answer key. Reject." — prediction-markets.md §5.1/5.2

- "the talk's demotion to 'byproduct' quietly removed the only deadline the project had" — blind-spots-fable.md G1, on the project's own purpose framing self-undermining its falsifiability

- "Entry 2 blocks justifying layoffs; it does not block manufacturing the conditions under which a layoff needs no justification. - flagship-osint-scoping.md: naming mismatch between the task's ORG field ('Rothschild & Co') and the researched dossier (Brompton Bicycle) — 'this naming mismatch should be confirmed with the requester', unresolved in-file
- flagship-osint-scoping-wave2.md: Wirecard's Braun criminal trial 'unresolved mid-2026' — 'a settled verdict would upgrade its mechanism-grading value and could revisit the Enron-vs-Wirecard ordering'
- flagship-osint-scoping-wave2.md ends without a final reconciled decision — two competing flagship/backtest recommendations stand in the same file (Netflix+Enron vs the contrarian pass's Netflix+Carillion-primary/Enron-as-control/NMC), no owner ratification recorded in this directory
- opportunity-cases.md: 'No sell-side research notes are in hand for any of the fourteen cases' — flagged as 'the single most fixable gap'; a Factiva/LexisNexis archive pass is named as the obvious next step, not done
- opportunity-cases.md: several named factual claims gated out as unverified (Royal Mail's '~12% vs 70-90%' automation figure; Cevian's 2013-2017 agitation dates; Rico Back HY2019 remarks) pending sourcing
- insider-risk-and-ethics.md: real-deployment guardrail (DPIA, LIA, Art.22 human-in-loop, bias testing) is specified but explicitly not yet built/gated in any system — 'the twin's design should carry Part B as enforced guardrail config' is a should, not a done
- misuse-catalogue-fable.md: ranks 5 most-needed catalogue additions (D2 strategic non-modelling, outsiders B1/B2/B5, exit-cost asymmetry A1, absence-as-alibi H1, unauditable constraint removal I1/I2) as unaddressed in the design it reviews — no confirmation elsewhere in this group that these were incorporated
- blind-spots-fable.md: 'If you fix only five things' list (break the closed validation loop; add a causal-model workstream; regime-split the £ engine; put the twin inside the twin; adopt external gates incl. a pre-registered forecast book 'starting this month') — presented as unimplemented recommendations at time of writing
- prediction-markets.md §10: 8 named 'honest gaps' including no Polymarket-specific calibration study at matched horizons (only Kalshi/cross-platform studies exist), the Manski [p²,2p−p²] uncertainty bound 'never applied empirically anywhere', and two 2026 arXiv papers read only as abstracts not full text
- risk-threat-sota.md: 'Honest limitation: cyber has too few tail events per firm for classical back-testing power' — acknowledged unsolved constraint on the recommended methodology
- tabfm-assessment.md: 4 explicit conditions for revisiting the REJECT verdict (technical report published; distributional regression head ships; licence permits production use; independent calibration benchmarking favorable) — none met as of the file's writing
- arckit-capability-map.md §3.3: 'Refresh is per-project, single-repo' — arckit's build/refresh harness doesn't natively span the org-of-repos estate; 'orchestrating N claude -p runs, one per repo, ourselves' is named as still-needed work, not done

**Reviewer notes:** This directory is a pure research/scoping corpus, not a decision-tracker or build-tracker in the ticket-tracker sense used elsewhere in the project (no 'Status: OPEN/CLOSED/RESOLVED' ticket headers). Its content is nonetheless heavily decision-shaped: several files are literally 'pick one flagship' or 'accept/reject this technology' analyses with explicit verdicts, so they are reported above under both decisions and tickets depending on shape. Two files (flagship-osint-scoping.md and flagship-osint-scoping-wave2.md) each contain a self-contradicting structure: a primary recommendation followed by an appended adversarial 'contrarian challenge' section (evidently written by a different reviewer persona) that substantially attacks the file's own headline pick. A reviewer relying only on each file's top-line 'Recommendation' section would miss that the file itself later reverses or heavily qualifies that recommendation. As of these files, no later document in this directory reconciles the two waves' conflicting Netflix+Enron vs Netflix+Carillion-primary recommendations into one final answer. blind-spots-fable.md and misuse-catalogue-fable.md are both explicitly adversarial/red-team review documents ('role: divergent reviewer', 'Written by someone who has not seen your code and does not want to') critiquing a design ('the twin', 'the settled framing', 'your eight entries [misuse catalogue]') that is NOT itself present in this research/ directory — they reference and critique an external map.md and an existing misuse catalogue that must live elsewhere (likely .scratch/twin/ or .scratch/twin/issues/). Their findings are therefore about a design this inventory cannot independently verify was ever changed in response. Confidence ratings ('high'/'medium'/'speculative') are used self-referentially throughout blind-spots-fable.md and misuse-catalogue-fable.md as an internal severity/certainty marker by the reviewer-author, not as any kind of externally verified score — worth flagging to a drift reviewer as author-asserted confidence, not measured confidence. prediction-markets.md is unusually disciplined about sourcing hygiene, tagging nearly every claim [reported]/[inferred]/[probe] — this is the strongest evidentiary file in the group and is worth treating as a template for how the rest of the twin's evidence-grading ladder (referenced but not defined in this directory) should look. tabfm-assessment.md and risk-threat-sota.md both converge independently on the same conclusion — that the project's core difficulty is calibrated inference under sparse/heavy-tailed data, not supervised tabular prediction — which is corroborating rather than duplicative. Two files (opportunity-cases.md and flagship-osint-scoping-wave2.md) both independently invoke the same methodological move: pairing a 'contaminated' (famous, LLM-memorised) case with a 'clean' comparator at matched evidence density to produce a 'memorisation leakage discount dial' (Ørsted/RWE pair mirroring the Enron/NMC pair) — this is a recurring, load-bearing design pattern across the group worth a reviewer's attention as a single idea appearing twice, not two separate ideas. No file in this directory carries an explicit date-of-last-update banner beyond its 'Date:' header (all clustered 2026-08-04 to 2026-08-05, with arckit-capability-map.md noting its own arckit version snapshot 'at investigation time (2026-08-04)') — no staleness markers or superseded-banners are present except the wave1/wave2 relationship already noted.

## twin-build-00-30

**Destination:** "Be careful to not allow scope to drop in this and prematurely declare things as done, and make sure we're prepared to always change our code and never be married to previous investments." (00-constitution.md, 'The standing guard'). Nearly every ticket in this range status-lines 'done' while still carrying at least one explicitly unticked acceptance criterion or a named 'Not built' section — the group's convention is that 'done' closes a ticket's slice, not the owning decision ticket's full ACs. ## govern-what-you-dont-control

**Destination:** "A workload nobody can modify is still governed, and still carries a policy version. COTS products, vendor images and anything else that cannot be made to wear a policy-as-versioned.dev/policy-version label are brought under the same versioned, priced, proportionate model as first-party workloads — reached by a policy-dependency shim at the infra decision point, or by policy describing them, or both. Reaching it means an unversioned workload has an answerable compliance state, a cage spec, and a place in the £, rather than passing freely because nothing matched it." (map.md, Destination section)

### Decisions

| Id | Title | Status | Gist | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 01 | Is "COTS" the boundary, or is it "can we change the pod spec"? | Type: grilling / Status: resolved / Blocked by: none | The axis is "can we change the pod spec," defined by its remedy: wrap it or shim it, never exempt, never deny. The population is mostly the platform's own infrastructure (five unlabelled third-party charts: SPIRE, Istio, OpenBao, Pomerium, Dex, plus flux-syste | .scratch/govern-what-you-dont-control/issues/01-what-is-the-real-bound |
| 02 | Where does the shim sit: procurement, build, or admission? | Type: grilling / Status: resolved / Blocked by: 01 | Three layered sources across the lifecycle: wrapper (packaging time, makes the product a real pinned signed dependency), procurement record (selection time, carries the declared version), and SPIFFE identity (runtime, checkable). A platform-side allow-list map | .scratch/govern-what-you-dont-control/issues/02-where-does-the-shim-si |
| 03 | What does compliance mean when the vendor image cannot comply? | Type: grilling / Status: resolved / Blocked by: 01 | Compliance is achieved by the cage, not the image; the composite complies — already true in code via cage-tier.yaml. Conditional policy decides admission, the tier decides tightness, and residual risk lands in the institution's own band but tagged.
