### Rejections and supersessions

- "A platform-side allow-list mapping workload identity → version at admission" was rejected: "an allow-list entry is the platform asserting a compliance claim about software nobody inspected, which is the unearned green this estate refuses." (issues/02-where-does-the-shim-sit.md)
- "there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified." — owner quote, map.md Notes, also repeated in issues/04 and issues/05
- "A separate budget was rejected as where uncontrollable risk goes to be quietly tolerated." — rejecting a separate budget for COTS residual risk, keeping one band instead (issues/03-what-does-compliance-mean-for-a-vendor-image.md)
- "A modifier on retain was rejected: recourse does not reduce the loss, it reduces the net after recovery, which is what transfer already expresses." (issues/04-vendor-recourse-as-transfer.md)
- "The exemption ledger was offered as an option here and is now banned outright (see the map's Notes). It is not where commercial terms live, and it is not where anything else lives either." (issues/04-vendor-recourse-as-transfer.md)
- "That is a good argument, and it is overruled: an entry naming one workload is a carve-out whatever properties it carries." — rejecting the exemption ledger's own defence (scoped/priced/expiring/versioned/owned) (issues/05-remove-the-exemption-ledger.md)
- Ledger machinery physically deleted: estate/platform/policy/ledger/exemptions.yaml, render-exemption.py, verify-exemption.sh — "only prose mentions remain (correctly describing it as removed)" (issues/05 Comments)
- estate/talk/verify-all.sh:25's "no ledger entry, no exception" beat (verify-exemption.sh) is removed; explicitly note line 24's "exemptions dissolve into conditional policy" beat (verify-conditional.sh) stays and its name "becomes literally true rather than aspirational" after the change (issues/05)
- "Any design requiring the upstream image or chart to carry our label is not a solution to this problem." — standing preference ruling out a whole design family (map.md Notes)

### Open items

- Whether the shim is admission-time, build-time, or procurement-time — map.md 'Not yet specified' notes this is only partially settled by ticket 02's three-source answer
- Whether 'wrap' and 'shim' are two mechanisms or one — sharpens once the shim-location ticket runs (per map.md, though ticket 02 gives a layered three-source answer, this framing question is left listed as not-yet-specified)
- What a COTS workload's compliance even means when the policy body assumes it can dictate the pod spec, for rules a vendor image may be incapable of satisfying (map.md 'Not yet specified' — though issue 03 substantially answers this via the cage reframe)
- How this lands in the £ precisely — an ungovernable-but-necessary product is retained risk; the estate has vocabulary (transfer, retain, constraint pre-filter) that may already fit (map.md 'Not yet specified', partially addressed by issues 03 and 04)
- Computing the semver bump is explicitly out of scope for this map, tracked instead in .scratch/computed-semver/map.md, though this map's answers change that map's corpus
- Ticket 05's consequence 2 ('sequence the cage producer before the OSCAL up-flow beat') was executed but the ticket's own numbered consequence list skips from 1 to 2 to 4 (no consequence 3 present in the source text) — an apparent gap/typo in the ticket, not resolved or commented on
- legacy-till's 'some workloads stop running' consequence remains undemonstrated by any live Deny fixture as of ticket 05's close

**Reviewer notes:** Map.md's "Decisions so far" index lists tickets 02, 03, 04, 01 in that order (not numeric order) — the map narrates them in dependency/discovery order rather than file-number order; ticket 01 is actually foundational (Blocked by: none) and the others depend on it, so map ordering reflects when each was resolved/surfaced, not logical precedence.

Issue 05's comment corrects its own earlier draft: an "earlier draft of this comment quoted the stale pre-diff 24/26" — the corrected, final figure is pass=23 fail=2 skip-live=3 (23/25 offline, not 24/26). This is a self-caught and self-corrected stale number, flagged explicitly in the source.

Issue 05 records two FAILs in the full verify-all.sh run (verify-honesty.sh missing a local feeds-signing-key.pem; verify-reach-secrets.sh hangs on an untimeout'd kubectl --dry-run=client with no reachable context) — explicitly called pre-existing environment gaps unrelated to this ticket's change, left unfixed as out of scope.

Issue 05 explicitly leaves the "some workloads stop running" consequence undemonstrated: legacy-till, the one concrete exempted workload, is NOT denied under current numbers (driftwood's £40k band cages it baseline, TCoR ~£14,952 residual) — so the ticket's own predicted failure mode has no live fixture proving it, noted as "not this ticket's job to force a Deny case."

No formal ADR files were found in this group's paths — all "decisions" here are grilling-type tickets (issues/01-04) plus one task ticket (issue 05), not docs/adr/ entries, though ADR-0006 is cross-referenced from within ticket 05.

The group's Notes section attributes several direct owner quotes with exact dates (all 2026-08-20), used as binding constraints: "there'll always be COTS products..."; "there must never be an exemption ledger EVER, this is a banned concept explicitly, everything is codified."; "wrap it or shim it"; "it runs in a cage that simulates it, nothing runs without a cage even if they're permissive."; "in the book balancing there may be opportunity to recover via litigation...".

**Reviewer notes:** 1) No decision tickets or ADRs live in this directory — it is 27 numbered build/implementation tickets only, no index/README, no thesis file. ADR-0006 is referenced but not present here. 2) No ticket records an owner answer at all, so depends_on_bare_agree is empty everywhere — these files carry agent-written status/comments, not owner Q&A. 3) All 27 tickets were re-statused on 2026-08-20 by audit ticket mo-02; the phrase "Status corrected from `ready-for-agent` to `done`" appears on most, meaning the board's prior state was uniformly wrong in both directions. 4) Ticket 14 contains two comments in direct conflict: the mo-02 audit comment ("the istio-csr/SPIRE CA bootstrap fix ... has not landed") is explicitly declared superseded by the mo-11/mo-04 comment beneath it, but both remain, and the "Reopened 2026-07-31" section header still says BLOCKED with an inline "(fixed 2026-08-20 — see below)" patch. 5) Ticket 15 has the same shape: the mo-02 comment says "nobody has live-proven an admitted pod actually receiving a `posture/vN` SVID yet", flatly contradicted by the Status line and the mo-11 comment. 6) Ticket 17's "Reopened 2026-07-31" block and its "Definition of done" still name `ENABLE_CA_SERVER=false + istio-csr` as the blocker — a diagnosis ticket 14 declares "always wrong" — and its mo-02 comment is also stale. 7) Count disagreement: ticket 17 quotes a live run of "27/28 beats"; tickets 25/26/27 discuss an offline count of 24/25 vs ARCHIVE.md's claimed 25/25; ticket 15's mo-11 cross-ref is titled "prove 28 of 28 live". Three different denominators (25 offline, 28 live) are in play. 8) ARCHIVE.md's green-estate evidence is identified as impossible-as-written (cites verify-all.sh output at a commit where the script did not exist) — the strongest fabricated-evidence finding in the group. 9) Ticket 25's bug is the single blocker chaining 25 -> 26 -> 27; it is a one-line fix knowingly left unapplied. 10) The repo's stated "house convention" is that an offline verify-*.sh PASS suffices to mark a ticket `done`; several `done` tickets (09, 13, 16, 18, 19, 24) are therefore offline-only despite prose claiming 'live'. 11) Ticket 14's cross-reference to "ticket 11" is ambiguous: it means .scratch/multi-org-estate ticket 11, not build ticket 11 (OSCAL up-flow). 12) Ticket 20 is the only ticket where the gap is declared "real, not just an environment limit" for scaffolding-vs-claim reasons; ticket 25 is the only one with a reproducible code bug. 13) File mtimes: 24 files last touched 2026-08-20 12:54, tickets 14/15/17 later the same day (17:02/18:29) — the identity chain is the only area edited after the audit sweep.

## twin-decisions

**Destination:** map.md Destination: "A rigorous, comprehensive **digital twin of an organisation** and an **anticipatory strategic-intelligence engine**: it senses *any* signal — external (quantum, memory cost, AI-model access, sanctions, East–West realignment, climate, supply-chain, M&A) and internal (people, morale, knowledge concentration, comp, working patterns) — maps it onto the org's Wardley/dependency landscape, **war-games the what-ifs (fast-forward · rewind · play)**, prices every impact *and every candidate response* in **one £ risk currency**, and recommends the **cheapest proportionate response wherever it lives** — an HR lever, a security control, a strategic play. Versioned, attestable governance is *one enactment arm*, not the point." Also: "Plan the whole thing to full ambition — everything is modelled. The demo scope is worked backwards from the plan, later." and "Built **skills-first, from basics**, on **real risk modelling and real Wardley** — nothing inherited from the toy prior." Done when: "the whole is charted comprehensively (all workstreams, seams, and explicit acceptance criteria), grounded in real methodology, with a worked-backwards demo slice — such that a build could start any track without re-deciding its basis." spec.md restates the solution as "A **digital twin of an organisation** — a typed, git-versioned knowledge graph spined on the Wardley value chain — plus an **anticipatory engine** that runs over it", with four distinguishing properties: "It is a weather forecast", "It can be proved wrong, and the proof is structural", "It treats contamination as a first-class threat", "It is an artefact to argue with, not an oracle". Spec header: "Status: ready-for-agent / Source: `.scratch/twin/map.md` (`wayfinder:map`, decision-complete 2026-08-05) and its 22 resolved tickets. / Scope: **the whole system.** Each resolved ticket's full acceptance criteria remain the yardstick."

### Decisions

**Reviewer notes:** 1) This group is 22 decision/research tickets plus a map and a spec at 'ready-for-agent'. It contains NO build record: every twin capability described is prospective, and spec.md says outright 'the twin is greenfield'. A drift review should treat all 'claims_built' entries here as either inherited arckit facts or map/spec insertions made later by the build effort.

2) Status-line honesty is mostly disciplined, with three exceptions worth checking: (a) ticket 06 is headed RESOLVED yet all six ACs remain unchecked '[ ]' and it still lists four unresolved verification items plus 'NMC Health ... Needs a verification pass before anchoring scores'; (b) ticket 11's sensor-gameability AC is ticked '[x]' while its own text says the position is deferred and 'remain a known, recorded gap'; (c) tickets 10 and 15 correctly use '[~]' partial markers and say so in their status lines.

3) map.md and spec.md have been edited AFTER the decision phase with build-era facts, so they are not pure decision-time documents. map's 'Not yet specified' now says ticket 15's sensor set and ticket 10's threat-model/misuse items are 'closed — build ticket 82 / 83' with concrete file paths, and spec user story 45 carries an inline '(Build ticket 39 finding: ...)' that NARROWS a decision-group choice — Wirecard, selected in ticket 06 as part of the low-contamination suite, is reclassified at build time as a second contamination 'high' case, leaving Carillion + NMC Health as 'this story's actual low-notoriety pair'. Ticket 06 itself was never updated to match.

4) Counts agree: map.md's 'Decisions so far' lists 22 entries, spec.md says '22 resolved tickets', and the issues directory holds exactly 22 files. But the map's index is ordered by resolution date rather than number (15 appears after 17, 18 after 19), which can read as gaps on a quick scan.

5) Ticket 01 has no acceptance-criteria checklist at all — it predates the AC convention used from ticket 06 onward. Tickets 02-05 (research) also have no checklists.

6) Several decisions are recorded as bare option letters from the owner with the rationale supplied by the writer — most notably ticket 22 Q1 '(b) AND (c), SEQUENCED TO CONCLUDE IN (a)', ticket 11 Q1 'b, then c', ticket 10 Q2 whose whole recorded human rationale is 'we need to demo it', ticket 10 Q4 '(a) ACCEPTED AS NOISE FOR NOW', and ticket 15 Q3 'human reviewed ... nothing missing from the table'. Where the owner spoke at length the quotes are substantive (ticket 09 Q1 on perspectival £, ticket 12 Q3b on the actionability horizon, ticket 14 Q2b on CI signing, ticket 20 Q2b the standing guard).

7) Three tickets resolved AGAINST their own hypothesis and the spec deliberately keeps them visible: TabFM rejected (16), opportunity cases shown structurally unreachable (19), the power-layer critique accepted rather than answered (15). spec.md: 'A plan with no negative findings has not been tested.'

8) Load-bearing self-imposed guards a drift review should test the build against: 'each resolved ticket's full acceptance criteria stay the yardstick', 'every capability carries a depth grade (stub/partial/full)' travelling at runtime, 'code is disposable — the decisions endure', the published does-not-do register, and 'only as-consumed scores'.

9) The Flux hypothesis is the single explicitly unfalsified assumption carried into the build — held as 'integral enabler unless proven otherwise', with the test recorded in ticket 22 and repeated in both map and spec. If the build shipped Flux without running that test, that is drift the decision record predicted.

10) One internal tension inside the decisions themselves: ticket 09/10/15 make the £ perspectival and contestability primary, while ticket 15's own adversarial pass concludes the perspectival £ is 'the tell' that converts a power asymmetry into a plurality of viewpoints — accepted rather than resolved. The group knowingly ships that contradiction.

## spikes-explorations

**Destination:** No single group-level thesis statement exists; each spike/exploration answers one named risk or precondition for the wider project (ADR-0001, ADR-0006, ADR-0008, ADR-0009, PRD, and the computed-semver / policy-composition issue trackers). Recurring framing: "the same versioned Kyverno engine that judges both planes at admission emits PolicyReports... No second validation engine" (spikes/c2p-real-job/README.md). The native-CEL exploration frames itself explicitly as standalone: "*Project: Policy as Versioned Code, on Flux. Standalone exploration — not woven into the existing docs.*" (explorations/native-cel-without-kyverno.md).

| cs-06-verdict | Does the rule-set delta need `extends` to be readable? (intra-policy DRY) | Verdict: a rendered-artefact diff is ENOUGH. A reviewer comparing 'decided' vs 'built' should read the two together, not independently — cs-06's verdict about inheritance not being needed applies only to intra-policy DRY, not to cross-party composition, which cs-06b affirms as real and missing.","cs-06b/README.md is unusually self-annotated with strikethrough corrections (~~old claim~~ -> closed by ticket N) documenting its own claims changing over time as later tickets closed gaps it originally listed as open; the current file state already reflects tickets 06, 12, 17, 18 as closing four of its five original 'what this prototype does not do' items, leaving only 'the rule-conflict refusal is untested across parties' still open.","Two spikes (c2p-real-job and c2p-validatingpolicy-oscal) are explicitly sibling/successor: c2p-real-job's README states its sibling was 'the mechanism spike (toy policies, proved C2P consumes CEL ValidatingPolicy reports at all — risk-retired)' and that c2p-real-job adds independent schema validation the mechanism spike skipped — read them as a pair, not as duplicate coverage.","The explorations/ directory contains exactly one file and is explicitly marked in its own text as 'Standalone exploration — not woven into the existing docs' — unlike the spikes/, which are tied to specific tracker tickets (ADR/PRD/issue numbers), this document has no ticket ID of its own and is a freestanding architecture-options analysis.","No decision ticket or ADR file itself lives inside spikes/ or explorations/ — all decision/ADR sourcing here is the spikes'/exploration's own verdict statements (self-contained spike verdicts) plus references out to ADRs and issue trackers that live elsewhere in the repo (not read as part of this group's scope).","No ticket in this group records a bare 'agree'/'yes'/single-letter owner response; all statuses found are the spike author's own verdict/finding write-ups, so depends_on_bare_agree is empty for every decision listed."]

## twin-build-61-92

**Destination:** "This batch (build tickets 61-91) is the closing phase of the twin project's build: it populates the honesty-of-scope machinery (affected-parties register, misuse catalogues, does-not-do register, depth grades that travel automatically with every artefact), builds and runs the three-subject demo (Royal Mail for falsifiability, Netflix for the whole engine and cross-domain £ comparison, Intel for a live unresolved forecast, plus Kodak/Maersk as a lighter portfolio tier), instruments and (attempts to) measure real-world Flux drift, and then works ticket-by-ticket down every remaining capability's unchecked acceptance criteria until, at build ticket 91, 'demo-slice' closes and the published capability aggregate reaches 73 of 73 across all thirteen shipped capabilities. Ticket 77 states the destination most explicitly: 'anticipation and provable falsifiability first, then proportionate versioned governance, concluding in the one-currency comparison — the most seductive and least self-evident claim, earned rather than opened with. Lead with the £ and the sharpest person in the room rightly asks where the number came from. The order is the argument: earn credibility, then spend it.' Running underneath the demo work is a second, harder thread — the Flux falsification test (tickets 64/65/70/78) — whose destination is explicitly to 'run the test rather than assume the answer' and which, honestly, never reaches a resolved verdict inside this batch: the probe was never scheduled, the coverage floor became permanently unreachable, and the owner recorded that outcome rather than fixing it."

### Rejections and supersessions

- "Neither solves the underlying asymmetry — the spec is explicit that no constraint here constrains power — but invisibility is a separate harm from powerlessness and this one is addressable." — 61

- "This branch is not an AWS product decision. Action-boundary monitoring has at least five independent implementations, of which AWS Dogwood (2026-08-06) is the newest and, on the evidence, not the best." — 65
- "A constraint at the tool-call boundary holds under composition. An absence does not." (rejecting the original 'no merge code path' as sufficient for propose-only) — 66
- "'A lock, not a proof. Python has no private constructor'" (quoting twin/options.py's own admitted weakness) — 66
- "Reconstructing history for the existing window — rejected... Fabricated synthetic samples — rejected... An LLM-generated adversary producing realistic-looking synthetic drift — rejected" (three mechanisms weighed and rejected before the forced-drift campaign) — 78
- "a synthetic result is never evidence the twin anticipates the world, only evidence that the detection machinery works" (quoting twin/planter.py's SHARED_PRIOR_LIMITATION, cited to reject an LLM adversary approach) — 78
- "Netflix cannot carry this beat: its story is famous, so a twin 'anticipating' Qwikster or the 2022 crash is indistinguishable from reciting it — the contamination pillar would undermine the very thesis the demo leads with." — 71
- "A demo whose thesis is 'we can prove when we're wrong' cannot be embarrassed by showing failures... which is precisely what the prior effort's thesis could not absorb when it had to soften an overclaim." — 72
- "The prior effort's failure was exactly this: a 'Live' label on a beat that was not live, caught late in adversarial review — because narrated honesty relies on someone remembering." — 77
- "Lead with the £ and the sharpest person in the room rightly asks where the number came from... The order is the argument: earn credibility, then spend it." (rejecting an opening-with-£ demo structure) — 77
- "the audit discovered no hidden defect here" / "Neither ticket was green and neither hid anything. The first draft of this section claimed 'both tickets were green throughout', and that is false" — ticket 70's own review retracting its first draft's overclaim about tickets 64/65
- "The first draft's guard could not fail" (two review passes found the ticket 72 guard's checks were tautological and rewrote them) — 72
- "the beat proposed the wrong response... printing a contradiction to its own audience" — a six-agent adversarial review overturning the first draft of ticket 74's beat
- "ethics-gate is classified `code` in CAPABILITY_INVENTORY here" — reclassifying a previously-claimed 'skill' as deterministic code, against the codebase's own long-standing 'six real skills' narrative, at ticket 90

This inventory covers 61 through 91 in full; there is nothing at 92 to omit.", "The Flux/drift thread (tickets 64, 65, 70's finding 1, 78) is the single largest unresolved honesty thread in this batch: instrumentation was built and even adversarially/confirmatorily audited multiple times, yet the crontab was never installed and the owner was directly asked and directly declined to fix it (recorded at ticket 70's audit). Every ticket from 70 onward through 91 carries the identical two pre-existing 'known, red' invariant failures (drift_window_is_actually_being_sampled, flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable) in its own evidence section, explicitly named as unrelated and pre-existing rather than fixed — this is a consistent, self-reported, un-remediated gap running through the entire second half of the batch.", "A running pattern across nearly every ticket from 79 onward: closing a capability to 'full' moves twin/capabilities/*.yaml's digest, which moves every artefact's embedded capabilities_digest pin, which breaks the committed golden-digest byte fixtures — each ticket re-blesses them via a gated 'twin verify --bless-goldens --authorise' command citing the decision ticket. This is disclosed consistently and does not appear to be corner-cutting, but a reviewer should note it is the same mechanical consequence recurring roughly ten times (79, 81, 82, 83, 84, 85, 86, 87, 88, 89, 90, 91) — worth checking whether any one of those re-blessings was rubber-stamped rather than genuinely re-verified.", "Ticket 90 surfaces a real, self-reported inconsistency the project chose NOT to fix: ethics-gate was reclassified from 'skill' to 'code' after genuine investigation, but the 'six real skills' framing remains unchanged in twin/skills.py's module docstring, skill-thresholds.yaml, five other skill modules' own 'Nth of six' docstrings, a test file's _EXPECTED_SKILLS, and .scratch/twin/map.md — described by the ticket itself as a deliberate, named, unclosed gap rather than scope creep. A drift reviewer comparing 'what decision ticket 20 says' against 'what the codebase's prose still says' will find a live contradiction.", "Several tickets (64, 65, 66) carry unusual 'not done' status lines that read almost as red-team self-corrections: 'instrumented, NOT MEASURING — corrected 2026-08-10', 'pre-registered, VERDICT PENDING', 'refusal built, PR CHANNEL NOT WIRED'. These are unusually candid for build-ticket status lines and should be weighed as genuine signal, not noise — the project's own convention (named explicitly at ticket 70) is that a non-done status names precisely what is missing rather than hiding it.", "Ticket 90's investigated tension and ticket 82's reversed tick (on twin-inside-twin AC5) are two concrete examples in this batch of self-correction working as designed — worth citing positively if the drift review is looking for evidence the honesty machinery actually functions, not just that gaps exist.", "Two dated real-world tech-evolution signals recur across multiple tickets as load-bearing evidence for build sequencing/scope decisions: AWS Dogwood (2026-08-06, evaluated and explicitly rejected for the enactment arm at tickets 65/66/69) and the Cowgill & Zitzewitz corporate-prediction-market study (cited at ticket 63 for the twin's own adoption-risk scenario). Both are treated as 'signals about the world', not merely product literature."]

## twin-readme-and-capabilities

**Destination:** "Most build tickets under .scratch/twin/ are closed; 64 is instrumented and measuring, not closed. One dated signal binds to a component; one scenario execution emits forecasts — plural; one recorded outcome scores them under proper scoring rules; any artefact recomputes from its own pins. Scoring is in the first slice rather than retrofitted, because without it we cannot tell whether any later capability helped, and because scoring dictates what every other component must record." The tool builds a governed 'digital twin' model repository — world layer + org overlays, typed graph, causal propagation, £ pricing gated by evidence grade, forecast scoring under proper scoring rules, and a propose-only enactment posture — with every artefact pinned, signed, and reproducible from its own pins.

### Rejections and supersessions

- "What survives is that a control modifying a named FAIR factor must be provably in force, and a signed pinned version makes that verifiable rather than asserted. What does not survive is the claim that versioned policy is how governance works: ... most levers are not code ... so if versioned policy were the shape of governance, the cross-domain comparison the whole £ engine exists for could not exist." (twin/README.md, decision ticket 18 Q2)
- "Graduated autonomy — auto-apply the cheap reversible things — was rejected because cheapness is computed by the twin's own £ model, which is explicitly never authoritative: the twin would be deciding its own leash length." (twin/README.md, decision ticket 18 Q1)
- "A Gaussian copula on the path outputs was rejected: it needs a correlation matrix nobody in this system authors, and it would impose a second dependence structure alongside the graph's own." (twin/README.md, propagation section)
- "Formal identification (do-calculus, back-door analysis) stays out of scope, per Q5's own text rejecting it as a blanket requirement." (twin/README.md, decision ticket 08 AC4)
- "This file previously credited build ticket 21's shared-ancestry correction ... with 'the free structural half' of Q5's confounder discipline. Ticket 21's own file disclaimed that directly ... which is what Q5 actually asks for." (self-correction of an earlier README claim, twin/README.md)
- "An early draft of this beat proposed the billing rebuild through this channel, which is code with a real enforcement point — the textbook policy case, printed under a means line that said the opposite ... that mistake fails rather than only reading wrong on screen." (twin/README.md, whole-engine beat section — a prior draft's miscategorisation corrected)
- "A first draft of this ticket got it wrong ... The hook was registered with the matcher Bash|.*[Mm]erge.*, and the suite only asserted that some hook mentioned enact_guard.py ... a tool named land_pull_request would never reach the guard." (twin/README.md, propose-only section — corrected registration bug)
- "AC 3's tick was challenged in review and survives with its boundary written into the checklist. The challenge is fair ... this code verifies neither the signature nor the force." (twin/README.md, graded enforcement section)
- "Wirecard does not [earn low-notoriety] — a bestselling book, a Netflix documentary and mainstream coverage the size of Enron's earn it contamination: high instead of spec story 45's shorthand grouping of all three as 'low-notoriety'." (twin/README.md — narrows/corrects an earlier spec assumption)
- "A note this file carried from build ticket 30 said ticket 25's subject would change to anchoring the pocket org's own perspective valuations instead of severity ... that pivot did not happen." (twin/README.md — explicit reversal of a previously stated intention)
- "Read the rest of this section knowing the instrument failed. The declared hourly crontab was never installed ... from 2026-08-16T05:00Z the 90% coverage floor build ticket 65 pre-registered is permanently out of reach." (twin/README.md, Flux drift section)
- "The confirmatory audit was not confirmatory ... if integration problems are discovered here, the early-detection design has failed, and that finding is more important than the fix." (twin/README.md, build ticket 70)
- "covert sensing is recorded as permanently-excluded, which the loader checks is not the deferral beside it" — banning covert sensing outright rather than treating it as merely deferred (twin/README.md, constraint set section)
- "the twin would be deciding its own leash length" — rejecting the twin's own £ model as authority over its own autonomy (twin/README.md)
- 'ponytail: a lock, not a proof' applied repeatedly (options.py pre-filter, enact.py's original propose-only criterion) — explicit acknowledgment that a structural absence/allow-list is a lock an author could still deliberately defeat, not a cryptographic guarantee
- ENACT_MODE 'Mode' note in enact_guard.py: "This layer's refusal was written for the twin operating on the world in the ordinary run of things. It is not free while the twin itself is under active, hands-on construction ... the mistake that prompted the instruction was an unverified subagent claim, not a missing human hand on the merge button ... Absent both, the default is development — merges and enactment pushes are admitted." (twin/enact_guard.py — a standing instruction from 2026-08-25 that narrows the original propose-only guarantee to 'operations' mode only, defaulting to permissive)
- _push_target() carve-out (2026-08-16): pushes to the twin's own repository are now admitted, narrowing the original 'the twin could not write to any remote' guarantee — "this is still a weakening ... Now it can publish its own model unattended, and only the world is gated. The owner asked for that, four times, and the accountability moved to them by that instruction rather than by an argument in code." (twin/enact_guard.py)

### Open items

- The Flux drift verdict (build ticket 65): continuous-state branch will resolve to `unmeasured` on 2026-11-06 regardless of what happens between now and then; point-in-time branch cannot be concluded either — probe was not fixed by owner's own decision
- No triple in this repository has been through the calibration.md procedure (build ticket 23) — the discipline is enforced as a document, not an authoring workflow
- twin propose emits a proposal but never opens an actual pull request — 'nothing in twin/ has touched a live repository' (build ticket 66)
- Layer 2 (enact_guard.py) is 'a net over the shapes a merge takes here, not a proof' — a differently-named wrapper or a hand-rolled curl call with a token is not matched; upgrade path is a scoped GitHub App token, not built
- Six cross-repository dependency pins are tag pins (movable), and no commit-line pin is live — 'pinned' currently means 'pinned to a movable name'
- An enforcement rung is a declaration checked against nothing running — no cluster is asked whether a control is actually enforced
- 'warn' enforcement rung has no realisation anywhere in the estate
- A move between enforcement rungs is versioned but its own git commit is not keyless-signed
- The enactment corroboration channel is declared, not verified — nothing checks a merged-change or payroll run actually exists
- The action-state loop has a read side (corroboration.state, build ticket 68) and its only consumer (pricing mitigation credit, build ticket 86) — but an enactment still carries no evidence about a proposition or world model; a forecast still does not branch on action state
- Two-architecture (x86_64/arm64) cross-machine determinism check has never run — declared and wired, unproven
- No signature is ever verified in CI — TWIN_SIGNING_KEY is unset there
- Sensing is a dead end for propositions/forecasts in the general case: no bound signal moves an inferred position or changes a forecast outside the mitigation-credit consumer built at 86
- Nothing calls twin sweep or twin gameplay-sweep on a clock/schedule — both exist unconditionally but require a scheduler that does not exist
- The forecast book has no live venue connection (Kalshi/Polymarket/Metaculus) — every module reads a caller-supplied fixture price/question series
- The pocket org's own perspective valuations (400000/50000) remain unanchored fixture numbers, unlike twin/severity.py's now-anchored standalone parameters
- The pre-filter's identification leg is self-reported: an option that omits the constraint it actually crosses survives, and no code can currently tell
- Evidence-grade history check runs only at `twin validate`, not at load, and does not follow file renames
- Signing proves possession, not identity — a shared HMAC key means anybody holding it can forge any role's signature; sigstore/gitsign upgrade named but not built
- The trade-off curve's mean-based default tie-break has never been stress-tested against a heavy-tailed outlier account
- Overlay-to-overlay (tenant-to-tenant) isolation has tests but no constitutional invariant, and the manifest may not grow a seventeenth without the constitution changing first
- decision ticket 07 AC5 (representation format reuse-vs-custom) is recorded nowhere in code
- decision ticket 14 AC2's third clause (agent signature carrying model version + config digest) is unmet because nothing here is produced by a live model yet
- ENACT_MODE currently reads 'development' (permissive) — the propose-only tool-call-boundary guard is not the active default in this checkout

**Reviewer notes:** [
"The document is extremely self-auditing: it repeatedly documents its own prior drift and corrects it in place rather than silently — the capability aggregate went stale at least twice ('32', then '35/64'); the opening banner went stale from 30/77 to needing correction to 41/77 after build ticket 34's audit, then again from 75/78 to 88/92 after build ticket 88's own recount; three capabilities and two ticks were found undercounted during one re-derivation round. This pattern is itself a running theme of the document, not incidental.",
"The banner's headline count (88 of 92 build tickets closed) is explicitly computed via `grep -l '**Status:** done' .scratch/twin/build/*.md` rather than hand-tracked, specifically because hand-tracking had failed three times before (ticket 77's find, ticket 88's own recount, and this file's own repeated corrections).",
"Two invariants are permanently red by design as of the current state: drift_window_is_actually_being_sampled (expected to cycle red/green between probe samples) and flux_coverage_floor_is_still_reachable (red since 2026-08-16 and staying red until the window closes 2026-11-06) — a reader running `./bin/twin verify` today should expect exactly these two failures, not zero, and that is asserted as the correct/honest state rather than a defect to fix.",
"The 'What is honestly built' table claims 73/73 across all thirteen capabilities at full — this is presented as an unusual, almost suspicious-looking state ('with every checklist complete, no artefact can show anything but full unless a real capability's checklist regresses first') and the document is explicit that this completeness itself needs to be treated with the same scepticism as every other claim in the file.",
"twin/ENACT_MODE currently reads 'development' (a single word, checked into git) — meaning enact_guard.py's decide() function returns None unconditionally (mode != 'operations' short-circuits at the top) and the propose-only tool-call-boundary refusal is NOT currently active by default in this checkout; it only activates when TWIN_ENACT_MODE=operations is set for a given run, which the harness invariant suite forces for its own test but the live default does not. This is a load-bearing operational fact for any drift review: the layer-2 guard's protection is currently switched off system-wide, by the repository owner's own standing instruction, pending completion of active construction work.",
"The pocket-org-worksheet.md is explicitly the one place in the whole system where a human-typed number is the authority rather than something refused — 82 lines, all matching per the document, with line 30's price figures having required an explicit human re-authorisation on 2026-08-07 after downstream gates made the original earlier-authored figure invalid.",
"The capabilities/*.yaml files' evidence text substantially duplicates and cross-references the README's own prose (each capability file's 'evidence' fields quote or closely paraphrase the same sections read above), so the two source sets are not independent corroboration of each other — they appear to be authored from the same narrative rather than two separately-derived accounts.",
"twin/calibration.md itself states plainly that it 'does not make an estimate correct... Calibration is a property of an estimator measured over many estimates, not a property of one triple' — an explicit epistemic caveat about the limits of its own procedure, worth noting given the README's own claim that zero triples in the repo have actually been through it yet."
]

## multi-org-estate

**Destination:** map.md, "# Map — the estate becomes genuinely multi-org, and genuinely green" (Label: `wayfinder:map`. Charted 2026-08-19): "The estate is **actually complete and actually multi-org**: `estate/talk/verify-all.sh --live` reports **28/28 green with no vacuous checks**, and the six institutions are **six real GitHub organisations** holding their own repos, consuming each other as signed, versioned, Renovate-bumpable dependencies over the real internet. The demo's claim \"six live organisations\" becomes literally true." And: "Reaching it means: the live identity chain works (tickets 14→15→17 of `talk-spec` close for real), every verify script fails when it cannot see, the split is done with history preserved, and the war-gaming scenario library is substantially expanded." Standing preferences: "Assume internet always. The old \"no venue-Wi-Fi dependency\" guarantee in `RUNBOOK.md` is a **declared false constraint** and is being removed, not worked around."; "Split preserves history (`git filter-repo`), and the hub **loses** `estate/`"; "Honesty over green. A check that passes because it could not look is worse than a red one; this effort has already found three." Execution is in scope: "The owner chose destination (b) — the estate actually complete, not merely a plan for it."

### Rejections and supersessions

- map.md: 'The old "no venue-Wi-Fi dependency" guarantee in `RUNBOOK.md` is a **declared false constraint** and is being removed, not worked around.'
- map.md: 'the hub **loses** `estate/` — the six repos become the source of truth, not a mirror of a monorepo.'
- map.md: '**`pitch-v5.mp4` is internal-only until the split lands.** It narrates "six live organisations", which is six directories and six empty orgs until then.'
- map.md: 'Honesty over green. A check that passes because it could not look is worse than a red one; this effort has already found three.'
- map.md / issues/13: 'benchmark only, and therefore no estate work: markets may grade forecasts, never price a control.'
- map.md / issues/17: 'genuine deadlock gets a **deferred** reconciler (which must resolve between obligations, never waive one — exemptions are banned).'
- map.md / issues/07: '`README.md:19`'s `nist`/`ico` → `platform` → institutions chain is **false** and must be corrected.'
- issues/07: 'estate/README.md:19 declares nist/ico → platform → institutions. In fact **all three institutions pin `nist` directly** ... and **nobody pins `ico` as a GitRepository at all**.'
- issues/07: 'Mandatory bundles would re-introduce through the back door the intermediation that was rejected for regulator pins — platform as a single point of failure for regulatory currency.'
- issues/12: 'Ticket 07 said these "become their own repos in the hub org... not directories inside the hub repo." This ticket keeps them as directories in this hub repo instead' — a named, deliberate deviation.
- issues/12: 'Inventing that scope unasked is the wrong kind of proactive here.'
- issues/12: '`estate/ARCHIVE.md` ... its checklist's `estate/talk/verify-all.sh` proof-block is now labelled as a **historical snapshot at a named commit**, pre-split, not a currently-runnable claim.'
- issues/12 addendum: 'point 6's "`twin/` never `open()`s an `estate/` path" claim was false, found by review, fixed here, not just re-asserted.'
- issues/12: 'ticket 07's "4 scripts" was counting the `.sh` entry points, not every module they import.'
- verify-08-filter-repo-split.sh header: 'the "file tree matches the hub's estate/<unit>/ tree 1:1" check below is now PERMANENTLY INERT, on purpose, not a bug to fix.'
- issues/09 addendum: 'it was not cut by a human running the manual `git tag -s` command this ticket printed, and it carries no OpenPGP signature at all.' — supersedes ticket 07's dual-sign decision in practice.
- issues/09: 'keeping a commit pin that traces back to a REST-API-created tag — the exact channel this ticket's hard rule bans — would have hidden the gap rather than closed it.' (a prior attempt is described as 'the rejected prior attempt' / 'illegitimately-sourced Ready=True').
- issues/09: 'The live clusters *were* showing stale `READY=True` before this ticket's work — a leftover from the rejected prior attempt ... so it kept reporting `Ready=True` for three weeks against a tag that no longer existed.'
- issues/10: 'the "Renovate commits must be git push..." requirement stated above is wrong, and it was wrong from the start. `enact_guard.py` ... has no reach into that surface at all, and never did.'
- issues/04: '**`talk-spec` ticket 14's own diagnosis is wrong and should be corrected as part of this.** It blames istio-csr. There is no istio-csr or cert-manager anywhere in the repo.'
- issues/04: 'Do **not** install istio-csr: ~1 hour this way versus 1–2 days, three extra deployments and four more images to pre-seed.'
- issues/01: 'This is the most on-thesis bug class in the estate: it argues governance tools lie by showing green ticks, and then shipped two of them.'
- issues/02: '25 of 27 tickets read `Status: ready-for-agent` despite the estate demonstrably working ... The board cannot be trusted as a picture of reality.'
- issues/02: 'ticket 27 downgraded to NOT DONE. `estate/ARCHIVE.md` cites `pass=25 fail=0` at commit ef84d16..., but that commit only ever contained tickets 01+02 ... The citation is unverifiable as written.'
- issues/03: '**The framing was wrong at the start.** "Organisation" appears nowhere in the estate's code, scripts or READMEs ... it was a word the demo invented and then overloaded.'
- issues/03: 'Renaming it was rejected: `org` is a field name in emitted artefacts and golden digests, so a rename churns the provenance surface for a vocabulary win. An undocumented overload is the bug; a documented one is a decision.'
- issues/03: 'a `roles:` field nothing checks would be a fourth [assertion that could not fail].'
- issues/11: 'the honest live count is 25/28 not 28/28' and '"not in doubt" is exactly the standard ticket 01 exists to reject, so this is named as unproven, not glossed over.'
- issues/11: 'both `mesh-base` and `posture` were completely inert since the day they were first applied ... The base-identity SVIDs the estate had been crediting to `mesh-base` were actually coming from the spire chart's own auto-created fallback.'
- issues/19: 'A gate that credited every commoditising defence by construction, whether or not the link were honest, would be exactly the unearned green this ticket exists to refuse.'
- issues/19: 'Editing `market-intel.json` in place was considered and rejected' — a claim cannot corroborate itself, plus the key-rotation blast radius.
- issues/15: '`wargamer.py:138` reads `risk.get("deployed_move", chosen)` — a *declared* field ... That is an assertion, not corroboration.'
- research/scenario-slate.md §2.1: 'I had this wrong, and the correction is worth more than the original argument.' — the researcher's own first structural argument rejected by the data.
- research/scenario-slate.md §2.1–2.4: regulatory regime shift, EOL cascade, physical/geopolitical and n-day weaponisation all refused a feed slot ('a calendar, not a diffusion curve'; 'wrong curve shape'; 'a modelling error, not a coverage gap'; 'it double-counts').
- research/scenario-slate.md §4.3: 'Verdict. Library only. No feed component, no `velocity`, no Wardley coordinate — because there is no defensible answer to "how fast is this moving right?", and inventing one to fill a row would be precisely the overstatement this document is supposed to refuse.'
- research/scenario-slate.md §4.1: 'That is not a deliberate two-framing design; it is drift between two files nobody cross-checks.'
- research/scenario-slate.md §6: '"Widen K to flip a move sooner" is unsafe.' and 'What ticket 06 *must* not do is treat K as a free dial.'
- research/scenario-slate.md §7: 'Wiz's s1ngularity blast-radius numbers, Parametrix's "$5.4bn" ... and every analyst market-share estimate are vendor or analyst figures and are **not used for any triple**.'
- research/scenario-slate.md §7: 'That Anthropic's threat-intelligence reports support this entry. They do not, and using them for it would be a category error I want on the record.'
- map.md Out of scope: 'Archiving the old `policy-as-versioned-flux` estate ... A separate cleanup, not on the route to a working multi-org estate.'
- map.md Out of scope: 'Policy-version inheritance, and computable semver bumps ... Genuinely good, genuinely uncaptured, and **not on the route to this destination** ... **Now charted as its own effort**.'
- map.md Out of scope: 'Building out the `twin/` project itself. Complete at 73/73 ... Reusing twin machinery is in scope; extending the twin is not.'

- mo-09: v1.0.0 tags carry no OpenPGP signature at all, so ticket 07's dual-sign decision (needed for Flux's spec.verify) is unmet; and the tags were cut by a workflow_dispatch of cut-release.yml, not the human git tag -s path the ticket printed.
- mo-09: GitRepository.spec.ref.commit is still commented out ('pinned at release'), so each unit's verify-reconcile.sh fails on 'GitRepository commit not pinned' — 3 of the 6 fails in mo-12's 22/28.

- mo-10: Renovate's bump commits still land via the Contents/git-data API despite platformCommit: enabled; root cause unknown, three leads listed; now a non-blocking curiosity.
- mo-10: the end-to-end proof the ticket asked for (cut a platform release → Renovate opens a bump PR → the gate runs on that PR) is not demonstrated as one chain; the gate was proven on a hand-opened PR.
- mo-11: the OpenBao/secret half of the flagship beat is only offline- and structurally-proven, never exercised live through a real `bao write auth/jwt/login`.
- mo-11 / mo-12: three standing verify-all fails — coexistence (require-nonroot fan-out never applied live), access (Pomerium pod never deployed), honesty (reflexive.py's signing_key_present checks the private key file).
- mo-12: talk/verify-all.sh --live is 22/28, against a destination of 28/28.
- mo-12: .github/workflows/twin.yml's paths: filter would not have run on the mo-12 commit at all — 'CI would not have caught this regression on that commit, full stop.' Widening the retrigger policy is named, not fixed.
- mo-12: verify/ and talk/ were NOT made separate hub-org repos as ticket 07 decided; the recipe is left for a later ticket.
- mo-06 / research S8: F4 — the `note` on pq-cryptanalysis resolving its never-cheapest declared `transfer` was not written; the corresponding item in map.md's 'Not yet specified' was not closed.
- research F3 / §6: whether cage in moves() should price the cheapest tier rather than the loosest-fitting one — 'should be its own ticket'; no such ticket exists in this group.
- mo-17 / mo-18: which obligation regimes bind to which institution/workload is unresolved; there is no per-workload obligation scoping, so no existing demo scenario was retrofitted to carry multiple sources.
- mo-17: the overrides/reconciler stage for genuine obligation deadlock is explicitly deferred, not designed.
- mo-19: whether enactment.json should itself be detached-signed for tamper evidence — left open with a `ponytail:` note naming the upgrade path.
- mo-19 / mo-15: wargamer.py:138's general self-declared `deployed_move` read is unchanged everywhere else in the estate.
- mo-02: the ticket-25 signing-key bug and the ticket-27 ARCHIVE.md citation were surfaced but not fixed (audit-only ticket); ticket 27's GitHub archive step is a human/admin action.
- map.md 'Not yet specified': the demo re-cut (pitch-v5.mp4's 'six live organisations' line and missing screenshots) — 'Explicitly deprioritised by the owner ("don't stress the video as scope").'
- map.md 'Not yet specified': the headline→coordinate classifier — nothing ingests a news headline and decides which Wardley component it moves.
- map.md 'Not yet specified': per-org CI — what runs where and how the cross-org version cross-check works with separate checkouts.

**Reviewer notes:** 1) The destination number 28/28 is never reached in this group: mo-01 re-derives 28 (25 offline + 3 live SKIP), mo-11 measures 25 pass / 3 fail live, mo-12 measures 22 pass / 6 fail live. Three different live counts, each attributed but never reconciled to the map's own destination, and map.md's Decisions index still records the destination as 28.
2) Offline count disagreement: mo-01 claims '25/25 PASS' offline while mo-02 (same day) says verify-all.sh is '24/25 offline beats, not 25/25' because of the reflexive signing_key_present bug; mo-19 quotes 'estate/talk/verify-all.sh: 25/28 pass'. The govern-what-you-dont-control decision recorded in map.md says offline-only is 25 beats not 26.
3) mo-10 quotes as a 'relevant known fact' that the org-level Actions-can-create-PRs setting is ON (matching the user's memory note), then its own live check finds it OFF on all three institutions. Directly contradictory within one ticket.
4) mo-12's Comments assert the v1.0.0 tag was 'gitsign+OpenPGP dual-signed'; mo-09's addendum, written in the same session, corrects this: 'it carries no OpenPGP signature at all'. mo-12's body was not updated — a reviewer reading mo-12 alone would believe ticket 07's dual-sign requirement is met.
5) mo-12's point 6 originally asserted twin/ never open()s an estate/ path; its own addendum says that claim 'was false when this was written'. The main text is left in place with an inline correction — a stale claim and its retraction coexist in one file.
6) verify-08-filter-repo-split.sh now permanently SKIPs its strongest check (file-tree 1:1 against the hub baseline) because mo-12 deleted the baseline. Documented as deliberate, but the script can no longer detect divergence between a real repo and what was split into it.
7) verify-09-repoint-flux-sources.sh explicitly does NOT assert Ready=True (reports it as INFO) — its header still says no v1.0.0 tag exists, which is stale as of mo-09's own addendum and mo-12.
8) Proposal-count numbers move between files without reconciliation: research §3.5 predicts 4 emitted risks → 3 drifts → 3 PRs; mo-14 reports 4 proposals total (driftwood 2, tuppence 1, ludlow 1); mo-06 reports 9 forward drifts → 9 signed PRs across '4 appetite-file institutions'. The 4-institution count includes platform, which mo-16 separately guarantees cannot count as a fourth institution — two senses of 'institution' in play.
9) mo-01's own scope counts disagree three ways in one ticket ('the other 28', 'the ~27 the ticket names', 'is 29').
10) Research finding F3 (cage tier selection non-monotone) was explicitly recommended as its own ticket and never became one in this group — the only one of F1/F2/F3/F4 with no ticket. F4 also remains unlanded despite mo-05 and mo-06 both naming it.
11) mo-04's claim 'verify-identity.sh, 27/28 live+offline checks green' uses a 27/28 denominator unrelated to the estate-wide 28-beat denominator used everywhere else — easy to misread as the destination number.
12) Ticket 14 is a task but its status line reads 'resolved' (grilling vocabulary) rather than 'done'; tickets 03, 05, 07, 13, 15, 17 are the grilling/research decisions, the rest are tasks.
13) map.md's Decisions index does summarise mo-12 as 'talk/verify-all.sh --live is 22/28 today', so the map is honest about the gap — but the destination paragraph at the top still states 28/28 with no banner noting it is unmet, and the destination's other clause ('six real GitHub organisations ... consuming each other as signed, versioned, Renovate-bumpable dependencies') is only partly met (Renovate PR creation blocked by the org toggle).
14) mo-10's status line and body say the pass was 'documentation-only ... no further code or config changes were attempted', yet section (b) describes shipping a new cosign signing chain to three repos and running two live test PRs in the same session — framing and described work do not agree.

## computed-semver

**Destination:** map.md Destination: "**A policy release states its bump and is refused if the evidence disagrees.** The release gate evaluates the candidate version and its predecessor against a corpus, observes how verdicts move on currently-compliant workloads, derives **major / minor / patch** from `CONTEXT.md`'s existing definition, and fails the release when the declared bump contradicts what was measured." Plus: "The same rule binds the **platform's own version**, not just the policy body — a platform bump changes the version array, which changes which policies run, which changes verdicts. The distribution layer does not get to exempt itself from the rule it distributes." Map title: "the version number computes itself". spec.md Solution: "A release states its bump. The gate measures the bump. The gate refuses the release when the two disagree in the unsafe direction." Standing preferences: "The engine must reproduce a human's correct answer before it is trusted to give its own." and "Honesty over green — a gate that passes because the corpus missed the case is this estate's favourite bug, and it has three already. Coverage must be stated, not implied."

### Rejections and supersessions

- map.md: "the bump is still an editorial judgement: faithful-floor's *Cut 2.0.0 + 2.1.1 releases exercising semver meaning* asserted each bump and then authored a fixture proving it. The proof is post-hoc justification, not derivation."
- map.md: "That same ticket records version-mechanics gaps found 'by review, not by CI', twice. This map inverts the flow."
- map.md standing preferences: "**Inheritance is not the destination.** ... Do not let a refactor of every policy file hostage the release gate."
- map.md: "**Build in the new `estate/`; mine the old faithful-floor estate for test material.** Its 1.0.0 / 2.0.0 / 2.0.1 / 2.1.1 line is signed, live-proved, and is the validation set."
- map.md out of scope: "**Rewriting the old faithful-floor estate.** It is being archived by the other effort. Read-only source of validation fixtures here."
- map.md: "Honesty over green — a gate that passes because the corpus missed the case is this estate's favourite bug, and it has three already."
- map.md on ticket 06: "**answers a narrower question than the map meant; see the [`policy-composition` map]**" and "The claim 'inheritance leaves this map' is **withdrawn** — that was the narrow reading."
- issues/06 Correction: "**This ticket's body wrote down the wrong question.**" and "The Answer below is correct **for the narrow question as written**, and it stands. It does not settle the real one."
- map.md on ticket 07: "**'unversioned' was the symptom; the disease is unpinned delivery.**"
- issues/07: "A version number on a file that no `GitRepository` pins is decoration, because ADR-0002's unit of adoption is the reviewed pin bump."
- issues/07 second reframe (owner): "**there is no gate, only cages** ... **Every policy that carries content is `Audit`.**"
- issues/07: "I first recommended splitting the five ... **The owner chose one mechanism.**" and "I also argued at first that per-version copies would race ... **That was wrong.**"
- issues/07 finding 3: the array's `action: "Audit"` field "is read by nothing ... **Delete it.**"
- issues/07: "Applying the `>=1.0.0` rules at `0.x` by local rule was rejected: cs-05 refused to invent a dialect of semver."
- issues/07: "A second policy family with its own claim was rejected: it needs a second label, and the one-string pin that doubles as the workload selector is the original's signature elegance."
- issues/07: "Running both copies side by side through a transition window was rejected" and "Redefining major to include enforcement removal was rejected, because it breaks the workload-side definition that is the hard half the estate already got right."
- issues/05: "**Drop the `feeds/sign.sh` shape for this artefact.** ... This ticket therefore removes a signing mechanism rather than adding one." — map.md restates it as "**removes the `feeds/sign.sh` shape ticket 04 chose**"
- issues/05: "**The override path: there is none.** An override carrying evidence, a signature and an expiry is exactly what `exemptions.yaml` carried. That argument lost on 2026-08-20."
- issues/05: "The owner rejected 'strictly greater than every existing tag'. Patching a still-supported older line is a normal version lifecycle. That rule forbids a legitimate backport." and "The replacement, 'exactly one increment with no gaps', was stricter than the specification."
- issues/05: "**Ship the gate hard and version the five first** ... A grace mode is a threshold wearing a different coat, and it never gets removed."
- issues/05 comment: "A gate that measures a set the clusters do not run answers a question nobody asked, which is the same defect section 9's first live bug describes at the institution end."
- issues/04: "This **corrects ticket 03's wording**. Its 'per CEL expression: satisfied, violated, absent' model only fits predicates."
- issues/04: "**No percentage.** A percentage invites a threshold, and a threshold invites tuning the corpus until it passes. That is the failure this map exists to kill."
- issues/04: "the precautionary escalation to major that this ticket's bullet assumed is the wrong instrument."
- issues/03: "discounting a workload because its claim is weak is an `Exemption` wearing another name, and `CONTEXT.md` bans that concept outright."
- issues/03: "**The corpus is not signed.** It is generated deterministically ... that proves the same property more cheaply than a signature."
- issues/03: "**A witness shape missing from the spine fails the corpus build.** The repair is to extend the generator, **never** to add the fixture by hand."
- issues/02: "**This is a change, not a description — and the code says so.**" (cage-tier.yaml's own comment contradicts always-caged)
- issues/02: the alternative reading of compliance "would make every new Audit policy a **major** bump and collapse the lane-keeping half of the thesis into the gate."
- issues/01: "the real tag's decimal ... doesn't follow textbook bump-and-reset semver either ... this is named as a real gap, not smoothed over."
- issues/01: "**If the engine cannot reproduce a bump a human already got right and proved, this map's destination is not reachable in its current form** — say so plainly rather than tuning until it agrees."
- issues/08 Correction (review): "The paragraphs above overstate the diff and contradict themselves ... Both cannot be true ... conflating the two let the ticket claim a deferral the shipped code did not make."
- issues/15: "The originally planned numbers (1.0.2/2.0.1) were deliberately renumbered to an honest major bump (2.0.0/3.0.0) after the gate itself proved the original numbers would have under-declared a real break."
- issues/16: "a deeper self-scoped-policy classification bug in `cage_engine.py` that had been silently making EVERY version bump on a self-scoped policy read as 'major' regardless of real content"
- spec.md Out of Scope: "**Repairing the four gaps the composition prototype found.** They are defects in the platform repo, found from the hub. That map names them and that repo fixes them."
- spec.md Out of Scope: "**Editing `CONTEXT.md` with gate jargon.** *Hole*, *proved exclusion* and *predicate expression* belong to one gate. `CONTEXT.md` is the thesis glossary."
- spec.md Further Notes: "**The gate cannot ship before the repair.** ... So the gate would refuse every release from day one for a reason unrelated to the release. That is the finding, not a defect."
- issues/26 / spec.md: "A renderer defect therefore appears as a refusal in the document. This is why the renderer does not get a second seam."
- map.md: "**The bump is a property of a composition, not of a file** ... cs-01's method is **extended, not unchanged**"
- map.md out of scope: "**Policy inheritance, of either kind.** ... This map takes one fact from it and no more."

### Open items

- map.md Not yet specified: "**Whether the cage gets a counter-pressure.** Cage severity is now the only enforcement, and it ratchets one way ... `verify/proportionality/render.py` prices a control against a risk band and is the only candidate. Nothing wires it in." (also issues/07 limit 9.1, spec.md Further Notes limit 1)
- map.md Not yet specified: "**Where a priced residual for a real workload comes from.** The tier axis is synthetic by design (ticket 03), and the estate holds two FAIR scenarios in total, both driftwood's. Nothing maps a pod to a scenario ... the cage half of the model is proved on synthetic input until it exists."
- spec.md Further Notes limit 2 / issues/07 limit 9.2: "The rule sees only the workload's side. Removing enforcement therefore scores as a patch."
- issues/20 and map.md: the six real infrastructure COTS witnesses (SPIRE, Istio, OpenBao, Pomerium, Dex, git-server) "were never available as real committed data to add" — a disclosed gap surviving shipment.
- issues/08: whether the permissive baseline should also apply to unversioned/COTS workloads — deferred to the COTS effort.
- issues/08: a fifth, genuinely no-op tier distinct from `baseline` — considered, deliberately not added, recorded and unresolved.
- issues/02 answer 5: the COTS/unversioned workload population spun out as its own effort ("it changes what 'verdict' even means for that population").
- issues/04 accepted consequence: "if CEL unreachability turns out to be impractical to prove, every exclusion is a declared hole."
- issues/03 limit 2: "Deny is unobservable at admission" — the bottom rung is proved by function test on `select_tier`, not by corpus observation.
- issues/03 limit 3: "A claim-less composite reports 'no cage spec'", kept open deliberately so ticket 08's spun-out question is not closed by omission.
- issues/07: "Whether `verify/proportionality/render.py` becomes the cage's counter-pressure. Named as the only candidate and left as limit 9.1."
- issues/07: "A viability model for a caged workload. Refused on purpose. It needs a threshold, and cs-04 banned thresholds."
- issues/15 with issues/25: the hand-classified repair release is supposed to be re-run by the generator standing check; no such re-run result is recorded anywhere in the group.
- spec.md: cross-party composition and DRY policy inheritance remain separate, unshipped efforts referenced from here.
- issues/09/10/11 remain `split` reasoning-only tickets — never closed as done in their own right.

**Reviewer notes:** 1) Numbering contradiction surviving into shipped state: spec.md, the map and tickets 09/15/16 all specify the repair release as platform `1.0.0` + policy `1.0.2` + policy `2.0.1`, and ticket 16 as a `1.0.1` backport. The shipped tags are `v1.0.0`, `policy/v2.0.0`, `policy/v3.0.0` (cs-15) and `policy/v2.0.1` from `release/2.0.x` (cs-16). Both tickets' acceptance boxes still carry the OLD numbers and are all ticked; only the Comments disclose the renumbering. Note also that `policy/v2.0.0` is described in earlier tickets as an already-existing cut tag, yet cs-15 lists it as a newly cut real signed tag.
2) Ticket 20's first acceptance box ("The witness set holds the five rederive fixtures and the six real infrastructure workloads") is ticked while its own comment says those six "were never available as real committed data to add".
3) Tickets 21 and 24 have every box ticked, yet ticket 16 later reports two foundational engine bugs (backport-predecessor selection in `gate_one()`, universal-major misclassification of self-scoped policies in `cage_engine.py`) were live and only found by proving a real release. Their "done" predates working behaviour on those exact criteria.
4) Ordering tension: cs-15's comment credits "the gate itself" with proving the renumbering, but cs-27 (which wires the gate into cut-release.yml) is Blocked by 15, and cs-15 is defined as hand-classified precisely because the gate cannot ship before the repair.
5) Count disagreements: ticket 11 and spec.md say `CONTEXT.md` gains TWO things; ticket 30 says three and ships three. spec.md's evidence table and ticket 18's table both list ten fields; ticket 04 says "nine fields, none optional".
6) map.md's header says the spec was "written 2026-08-22 from the seven resolved tickets" — six tickets carry Status: resolved (02-07); 01 and 08 are `done`. The count depends on which are being counted.
7) map.md says "Tickets 12–30 are all `done`" — accurate for 12-30; 09/10/11 are `split` and 01/08 `done`, so 30 tickets exist but only 19 form the implementation set.
8) Ticket 08 carries a self-authored Correction (review) admitting the original completion comment overstated the diff and claimed a deferral the shipped code had not made — the strongest self-reported honesty flag in the group, and it was fixed rather than left standing.
9) Ticket 29 has no evidence of its own; it entirely inherits ticket 28's commit list. No rendered Renovate PR body is cited, and no live adopter-gate run is claimed anywhere — the only live proving runs claimed in the whole group are the two publisher-side releases (cs-15 repair, cs-16 backport).
10) Ticket 13's atomicity criterion is self-weakening by design ("or the workflow states plainly that it cannot promise this") and the comment does not say which branch shipped.
11) Almost none of tickets 12-30 name a runnable verify command; evidence is commit SHAs. Runnable commands appear only in the early tickets: `verify-rederive-bumps.sh` (01), `verify-graded.sh` and `kyverno test .../tests/cage-tier` (08), `spikes/cs-06-inheritance-vs-diff/run.sh` (06), and the planned `verify-computed-semver.sh` (05).
12) The gate's offline twins inherit the `verify-rederive-bumps.sh` convention of SKIPping with exit 0 when the Kyverno CLI is absent — a green exit can mean "not run".
13) Ticket 06's Answer headline ("**No. A rendered-artefact diff is enough. Inheritance leaves this map.**") directly contradicts its own Correction, which withdraws that claim; both remain in the file, and the map records the withdrawal.
14) JSON written to /private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-cns-httpdocs-controlplane-policy-as-versioned-flux/417e2917-726b-46c9-9117-7b880114f08b/scratchpad/inventory/computed-semver.json

## policy-composition

**Destination:** map.md Destination: "**A party's effective policy set is composed from other parties' signed, versioned artefacts, and the composition is refused when it does not hold together.** Policy as a dependency you *extend and mash up*, not only pin. A regulator publishes **controls**; a publisher ships **implementations**; an adopter inherits both and adds its own. Pricing and threat parents contribute no rules but move the £." spec.md title: "a party's policy is composed from its parents, and refused when it does not hold"; spec.md scope: "**the composition step on the adopter side**, from the adopter's pinned parents to a signed composed artefact, plus the inputs the other parties must publish for it to run." map.md label `wayfinder:map`, charted 2026-08-21, "split out of `computed-semver` ... after its ticket 09 grew a compliance architecture that had nothing to do with computing a bump."

Whether anything reached a real remote is unresolved across the files. 3) SELF-DECLARED FALSE CLAIMS: ticket 10's Answer opens with a correction that its own id-fix paragraph was false (never applied, only described as done). Ticket 17 carries two corrections, the second being that `wargamer.wargame_cage_tier()` "never existed anywhere in the codebase" despite a detailed built-description and a "Review gate: PASS" line. A drift reviewer should treat unverified "built" prose in this group as suspect by the group's own precedent. 4) ACCEPTANCE-BOX INCONSISTENCY: tickets 16 and 17 have all boxes checked `[x]`; tickets 09-15 and 18 have every box unchecked `[ ]` despite being marked resolved. 5) COUNT/CLAIM DRIFT: spec.md and ticket 18 both assume "the first composition records ... three ungoverned namespaces" — ticket 15 records that this never fires on the real estate (ticket 11 landed the label first, so the real count is zero); map.md ticket 18 then reports exactly ONE genuine ungoverned namespace (`tuppence-reset`). 6) The 285-hole figure is consistent throughout (MODERATE's 287 minus ac-6/cm-6), but note ticket 14 states coverage counts "no claim" not "no valid claim", so both platform claims count as coverage even while one of them is dangling and separately refuses. 7) map.md says "The bump is a property of a composition, so compute it after composition" is the only line this map owes `computed-semver`; do not expect bump logic here. 8) `.scratch/0001-Move-the-general-version-comments-so-a-sunset-retire.patch` is unrelated to this group's content: a fleet-repo YAML comment-relocation patch authored 2026-08-15 (before tickets 09-18), citing yq v4.53.3 verification and Fleet PR #67.
